## Sixth Chapter.

### Concerning the Operations that occur in this Philosophical Art.

So that you may well grasp the operations, you must know that the philosophers observe three distinct periods of time and ascend from one to the other through seven degrees <term>degrees: stages of intensity in heat or specific steps in the alchemical process, often aligned with the seven classical planets</term>. In the first, the matter is worked in its raw state; in the second, it is cooked; and in the third, it is purified. In all three, however, this occurs without any foreign addition; instead, everything happens from, in, and through the matter from the beginning to the end, with one and the same fire—and indeed of the very lowest degree—in one furnace. And because this is a work of Nature, who for the most part acts by herself from beginning to end (except that you lend her a hand therein by separating the impure and her adverse parts, and by uniting the pure and uniform parts in the proper measure and proportion, and placing them into a vessel suited for her, with admini- <note>The text ends mid-word; likely "administration" or "administering."</note>

### Chapter Six.

...[wea]ker, the other stronger, one quite strong as the lion is, and the like. Now, my child, why do you want to overthink things so much, since you see that nature accomplishes such diverse and great things in such a very simple way? It is certain that if this art were a hundred times more difficult and artificial, a hundred people would obtain it sooner than they do in its simplicity. Listen, my child! I want to give you some advice. Study—yes, study, reflect, and contemplate with the utmost diligence and subtlety, but not, as almost everyone does, on high, subtle, and artificial things, such as adding or inventing yet another furnace to those described by the philosophers <note>original: "Philosophis"; referring to the established alchemical authorities of the past</note>, or another lamp to the fire, or other vessels (both earthenware and glass of various shapes and sizes) to those already known, or adding further operations to the existing ones. Instead, study how you might do away with the various furnaces, vessels, and manifold operations described by the philosophers, and how you might understand them all as one single furnace, fire, vessel, operation, and matter <term>matter: the *materia prima* or the fundamental substance an alchemist seeks to transform</term>. 

Only then, and not before, may you trust yourself to understand the philosophers; this is a sacred truth. However, so that I leave nothing untouched and maintain the loyalty and affection I promised you, I must make a few remarks regarding this.

Regarding the vessel: finally, in addition to the one that is earthenware and round, another is needed in the form of an egg-shaped glass <note>the "Philosophical Egg," a sealed glass flask used for long-term heating</note>; also, the first may be doubled for the sake of acceleration.

# Of the operations that [take place] in this, etc.

Take three parts water and one part earth. Know, moreover, that nature also has her own weight <note>This refers to the "weight of nature," or the specific proportions required for a reaction to occur, as opposed to mere physical weight.</note>, beyond which you cannot step; you cannot do too much even if you wished, though you can certainly do too many things poorly. For example: take a glass full of water and put salt into it; the water will dissolve it so that you see nothing of it. You may add more and more, but as soon as the water is sufficiently **saturated** <note>original: "saturiret"</note> and has taken its allotted portion into itself, the water will let the additional salt fall to the bottom and will take in no more, no matter how much you add to it. In the same way, you must observe nature in all things, for she can teach you best.

Lastly, I recommend to you **cleanliness** in all your works, so that you properly purify your materials—which must be pure—both from their **phlegm** <term>phlegmate: the watery, tasteless, and inert substance separated during distillation</term> as well as from **combustible earths** <note>Likely referring to "sulfurous" impurities that cause charring or "burning" of the matter.</note>, through repeated distillation. You must handle your **fixed spirits** <term>fixe Spiritus: the non-volatile essences that remain stable under heat</term> with great care, so that you do not drive out their spirits along with the phlegm. Rather, treat them with a gentle fire and pay close attention when they appear like clouds, so that you may catch them separately.

Now nothing remains for me but to wish you luck and the blessing of God, that He may grant it to you; with the attached plea to you, my children: that you may not misuse my fatherly love and faithfulness, which I have shown to you, but rather keep with you what I have written down here for you, so that instead of

## Chapter Seven.

### Recapitulation of the Entire Work.

Listen, you children of men and lovers of this **holy art** <term>holy art: a common term for Alchemy, emphasizing its spiritual and divinely-inspired nature</term>: why do you labor here in things of and from the earth? In so many minerals and metals? We have only two, which contain all others within themselves and descend from above; a **heavenly fire** is our **gold**, a **heavenly water** is our **silver**, and these joined together constitute our **heavenly mercury** as the third (1). We have nothing more, nor do we need anything more; and yet in these we have all other seven, namely **Saturn** <note>Lead</note>, **Jupiter** <note>Tin</note>, **Mars** <note>Iron</note>, **Mercury** <note>Quicksilver</note>, the **Moon** <note>Silver</note>, **Venus** <note>Copper</note>, and the **Sun** <note>Gold</note>, as well as our entire art repeated three times over. But because of this very origin, they are **living metals**, not dead ones like those of the earth: for just as there is light and life in the former, so there is darkness and death in the latter. How then can you be so foolish as to seek and labor for life and light—or the brilliance to enlighten and **transmute** <term>transmute: the alchemical process of changing a base metal into a noble one, like gold</term> other imperfect metals—within or from the dead and dark metals?

### Recapitulation of the entire work.

I will not even speak of the vegetable and animal kingdoms. Since I have already called you foolish regarding the mineral kingdom, I do not know what name I should give you here. For it is incomprehensible how a person can be so arrogant as to approach such a great, wise, and holy work without yet knowing that the great, wise, and most holy God is not a God of disorder, but rather of the most perfect order and harmony in all His works. Since they wish to make the **Philosopher’s Stone** <term>Philosopher’s Stone: a legendary alchemical substance sought by practitioners to transform base metals into gold and achieve spiritual perfection</term> out of plants, even though such things exist only for the preservation of animals; or out of animal substances, which, along with the former, exist only for the preservation of mankind; but man alone was created for the sake of God, so that he might soar toward Him through his love (m) and, after death, obtain his inheritance and praise and glorify Him eternally in His works.

Nevertheless, there are not a few who labor in these things, especially in the latter <note>referring to the animal kingdom</note>, where they are not ashamed to work with sweat, saliva, and even semen. They do not take care to notice that it is God’s order that man should maintain his health, yet sweat driven out by force and without necessity is harmful to human nature, and saliva given off in large quantities in the same manner is a hindrance to the **digestion** <note>original: "Dauung," an archaic term for digestion</note> of the stomach.

---

(m) 1 John 3:14; Chapter 4:16, 17. <note>original: "1. Joan. 3, 14. C. 4, 16. 17." These New Testament passages emphasize that "God is love" and that those who live in love live in God.</note>

<summary>The author rebukes alchemists who attempt to create the Philosopher's Stone from biological or human waste, arguing that such practices ignore the divine hierarchy of creation. He emphasizes that God is a God of order and that human life is intended for spiritual devotion rather than the manipulation of "unclean" substances.</summary>

# Repetition of the Entire Work.

...to increase the little spark <note>original: "Fünflein," which is an OCR error for "Fünklein" (little spark); a common mystical concept referring to the remnant of divine light within the human soul</note> of divine light or love (p) within oneself, so that it gradually breaks out into a flame, entirely purifying and renewing the body in order to possess the eternal kingdom. Therefore, turn your eyes away from the earth and lift them toward the heavens; contemplate the sun and its planets well, but with the eyes of the intellect. Study, reflect, and work in your mind until you have fully grasped the power, majesty, and benevolence of this great body of light, along with its effects and operations, so that perhaps it may cast a ray of its light upon you—

### Seventh Chapter.

...be cast, through which light you may also recognize the light on this earth, which (r) <margin>John 1:5</margin> dwells in darkness; after which you will immediately understand the **Hermetic saying** and those of other philosophers: (*that which is above is like that which is below; its father is the sun; its mother the moon; the wind has carried it in its belly*). <note>This is a foundational quote from the *Emerald Tablet*, a legendary text foundational to alchemy. It describes the "Correspondence" between the spiritual and physical worlds.</note> And not without reason, for the wind or air is like a carriage, by means of which all good comes down to us from above, as we require it. But although such good is found in all individuals of the whole world, it is nonetheless not useful to the philosopher, except in a single thing where he finds it in abundance—namely in our **gold**, which has its origin from three <note>likely referring to the alchemical "Tria Prima": Mercury, Sulfur, and Salt</note>, and is wrapped in a **Saturnine mineral**. <term>Saturnine Mineral: In alchemy, this often refers to lead or antimony, dense materials associated with the planet Saturn and seen as the "raw" starting point for creating the Philosopher's Stone.</term>

If you then wish to set to work, you must know how to release such **philosophical gold** <note>Philosophical gold is not common metal, but a spiritualized or purified substance extracted from "base" matter.</note> from its bonds and the mountain <note>original: "Berg"; used here metaphorically to describe the raw, unrefined state of the ore</note>, to separate the pure from the impure, to extract the subtle, and to turn that which has been extracted back inward again. If you have the right knowledge of the matter, you will easily know how to do this of your own accord. Nevertheless, because not everyone knows how to handle minerals, I will—for the sake of you children of the **Hermetic Philosophy**—explain this in some detail.

Take therefore from this **Saturnine Mineral** <term>Minera Saturnina: In alchemy, this refers to an ore of lead or antimony, named for the planet Saturn, which was associated with the heaviest, "basest" metal. It is the starting material for the transformation.</term> a quantity as you please; yet note that, because of the frequent purification, you should not take too little, for otherwise too little might remain for you; yet also do not take too much, whereby you would be hindered from successfully reaching the completion of the work. Then, through the administration of the external fire, its internal fire as well as its **mineral water** will begin to move and work into one another, so that it first completely releases itself and lets out its interior. This you will then recognize when you see your **gold** floating on its water like a **silvery mass**, from which upper part you must draw off its **impure film** <note>A thin layer of oxidation or residue that forms on the surface of molten or dissolving metals.</note> that will be found upon it, and you must take away the **earthiness** <term>Terrestität: From the Latin "terrestitas," referring to the coarse, solid dregs or "dead" matter that settles at the bottom during purification.</term> still settled under the water on the bottom of the vessel; then once more together on a gentle fire

# Chapter Seven.

Unite [the substance] through long rubbing, just as one is accustomed to making a good **amalgam** <term>Amalgama: A mixture or blend, traditionally of mercury and another metal, but here referring to the perfect union of the alchemical components through physical grinding.</term>. If it has stood for a while and you see no uniformity within it, rub it once more. Do not let yourselves be vexed by doing this for so long until you have a thoroughly uniform **mass** <term>Massa: The physical substance or "body" of the alchemical matter during its processing.</term>; then commit it once more to the fire and let God and Nature work. In due time, you will recognize the true union and **coagulation** <term>Coagulirung: The process where a liquid substance thickens and becomes solid.</term> by its **blackness** <note>This refers to the *Nigredo* stage, a phase of "putrefaction" where the matter turns black, signaling the death of the old form before it can be purified.</note>.

When you have further purified this mass through the **secret fire** <note>The "secret fire" is a central alchemical mystery; it refers to a non-burning, internal heat or a specific chemical agent that transforms the matter from within.</note> and repeated grinding until it reaches a **whiteness** <note>The *Albedo* stage, representing the washing and purification of the matter.</note>, you have then placed it into the state of a **golden magnet**. Just as a common magnet draws iron to itself, so this magnet draws its own kind—namely, gold—to itself.

Therefore, you must have at hand a quantity of such fluid, fiery gold, which you have previously prepared from the same **mineral** <term>Minera: The raw ore or source material from which the essence is extracted.</term>. By means of this, you must frequently **dissolve** <term>solviren: To turn a solid into a liquid state using a solvent.</term> and again **re-coagulate** this fixed and thickened gold as its own kind. Continue with **dissolving**, **grinding** <term>trituriren: To reduce to a fine powder by friction or grinding.</term>, and **coagulating** until such a magnet is saturated and will accept no more—until the **Green Lion** has devoured the **Red** <note>The "Green Lion" often symbolizes a strong mineral acid or solvent, while the "Red Lion" represents the fixed sulfur or the "soul" of the gold. Their union is a critical step in the "Great Work."</note>, or the **Dragon** has eaten its tail <note>A reference to the *Ouroboros*, an ancient symbol of a serpent eating its own tail, representing the cyclic nature of distillation and the eternal return of the matter to itself.</note>. Through this, it becomes as golden and rich in blood as a **Pelican** <note>In alchemy, the "Pelican" refers to a specific type of circulating vessel where the liquid drips back onto the solid, but it also symbolizes the Philosopher's Stone "feeding" its creation with its own essence.</note>, so that it will then richly reward your labor—though a long time will pass in the doing.

Now, since during this operation some impurities and **earthiness** <term>Terrestitäten: The "earthy" or dross-like impurities that remain in the substance and must be separated from the pure essence.</term> still [remain] with...

...have entered into it, then you must see to it, and apply all your diligence and attention to how you might once again free such things from all earthly matter and superfluities; to obtain this, you have highly purified it and brought it through its own **mineral water** <note>In alchemy, "mineral water" usually refers to a specific solvent or "mercury" derived from the ore itself, not common water.</note>, or you can do this through the separation of its principles **through distillation** <note>original: *per Distillationem*</note>, because it has already been brought so far that it has become a **golden vitriol** <term>Vitriol: While in modern chemistry this refers to a metal sulfate, to the alchemist it represents a purified salt or "green lion," a potent substance for dissolving and transforming metals.</term>; from which you will then easily separate its white as well as its red spirit, free them from all **phlegm** <term>Phlegm: The tasteless, watery, and inactive portion of a substance that remains after the active "spirits" have been distilled off.</term>, reunite them with its purified body, and be able to bring them into a **golden liquor or mercury** <note>original: *güldischen Liquorem oder Mercurium*</note>.

Of which you then once again **amalgamate** <term>Amalgamate: To physically mix and unite the substance with gold until they form a single, uniform mass.</term> three parts with one part of gold, **coagulate** <note>original: *eincoaguliren*; to thicken or solidify a liquid into a solid mass.</note> them in the fire of nature, as in the beginning, and let them run through the **seven planets** <note>This refers to the substance undergoing seven stages of refinement or "rotation," often associated with the seven traditional metals: lead, tin, iron, gold, copper, mercury, and silver.</note>, and you will then obtain such a fluid and fixed gold, which flows in the heat like wax, without effervescing or rising, but immediately solidifies in the cold, and will be transparent and clear like a beautiful gum, with which you can then work further and **multiply** <term>Multiplication: The process of increasing the quantity and potency of the alchemical medicine or stone.</term> according to your pleasure; only take heed in everything of the numbers three, five, and seven, and also, if it pleases you, the tenth.

See, here you have in a **compendium** <note>original: *Compendio*; a concise summary or shorthand.</note> everything that so many **philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophi*; in this context, "philosophers" refers specifically to alchemists who studied the secrets of nature.</note> in entire thick books...

## The Second Chapter.

Yes, I say: when we take into consideration this and much more which stands recorded in Holy Scripture regarding the blessedness of the lovers of Wisdom, we find that it is so great that no pen can describe it. We would indeed have to be more than foolish if we did not resolve, as Solomon did, to love her, to seek her, and to risk everything just to enter into her kinship and acquaintance, so that through her we might become blessed and most happy in both time and eternity.

## The Third Chapter.

### Through Which Means and Ways Wisdom is to be Attained.

Perhaps, by the grace of God, someone reading what has come before might be moved to also strive after Wisdom, and might therefore ask: By which means and ways is she most easily acquired? To such a person we answer with the words of the Apostle James, where he says in Chapter 1:5: *If any of you lacks wisdom, let him ask of God, who gives generously to everyone and does not reproach, and it will be given to him.* And this is thus the first and also nearly the only means which the wise have used at all times to arrive at Wisdom, according to their own testimonies. For thus writes Sirach <note>The Book of Sirach, also called Ecclesiasticus, is a book of ethical teachings from the 2nd century BCE.</note>: *When I was still young, before I was led astray, I sought Wisdom without shame in my prayer. I lifted my hands toward heaven, and then my soul was enlightened through Wisdom, etc.* And Solomon <note>This quote is from the Wisdom of Solomon 7:7, an apocryphal book traditionally attributed to King Solomon.</note> writes: *Therefore I prayed, and prudence was given to me; I cried out, and the spirit of Wisdom came to me.*

It is, however, well to note here that this prayer for Wisdom should, first of all, not be mere lip-service <note>original: 'Mund-Geschwätz'; literally 'mouth-chatter,' referring to insincere or superficial prayer.</note>, but must come from the whole heart; for Solomon writes of this in Proverbs 2:3–4: *If you with diligence after it* <note>The sentence ends abruptly here, continuing onto the next page. It refers to the biblical command to seek wisdom as if searching for hidden treasure.</note>

We must also follow this example and command of the Savior if we wish to attain true spiritual prayer and, through it, true Wisdom. For God is a spirit, and those who worship Him must worship Him in spirit and in truth. John 4:24.

### Chapter Three.

From what has been reported here concerning prayer, one may already partially understand the nature of **seeking**, which is required as the second means for attaining **Wisdom** and all that is good. As God Himself speaks in Jeremiah 29:13: *If you seek me with all your heart, I will let myself be found by you.* And Wisdom speaks: *I love those who love me, and those who seek me early shall find me.* <note>Proverbs 8:17. In the context of this era, "seeking early" often implies both seeking in one's youth and seeking with priority and eagerness.</note> And Christ the Lord says: *Seek, and you shall find,* etc. <note>Matthew 7:7.</note> Because so much depends upon this, it will not be unhelpful if we treat it a little further.

That the word **seeking** signifies as much as striving to obtain a thing that one does not have is known to everyone. The nobler and more precious a thing is, and the more we love it, the greater is the **earnestness** and **diligence** <note>original: 'Ernst und Fleiß'; these were key virtues in Pietist spirituality, emphasizing that spiritual growth requires active, disciplined effort alongside divine grace.</note> that we employ in seeking. And since **Wisdom** is the most noble, most precious, and most lovely good—in the absence of which we are most miserable, but in the possession of which we shall be and remain most **blessed** in time and eternity—we must necessarily seek her with the very greatest earnestness and diligence. And so that this may happen, and our earnestness and diligence, as well as our longing for her, may be all the more stimulated, we must (1) represent her to ourselves in our heart and mind,

For certainly, whoever loves God and His Wisdom—or Christ—also keeps the commandments; where one keeps the commandments, a holy life is certain. But whoever leads a holy life is close to God, and such a person fears nothing more than offending God, and he would rather die a thousand times than do even the slightest thing against Him knowingly and willfully; which is then precisely the **pure fear of the Lord**, which is so greatly praised and commended to us in Holy Scripture, as it says in Psalm 111:10: *The fear of the Lord is the beginning of wisdom.* <note>original: "Psalm CXI. 10." This is a foundational concept in the Bible, suggesting that true knowledge starts with a posture of humility and reverence before the Divine.</note> *The fear of the Lord furthers life and increases one's days.* (Proverbs 10:27, 19:23). *The fear of the Lord is the right worship of God; a blessed garden; a crown of wisdom; a source of life.* (Sirach 1, etc.) <note>The "Book of Sirach" (also known as Ecclesiasticus) is a book of the Apocrypha, highly valued in the 17th and 18th centuries for its practical and mystical advice on living wisely.</note> All of which we wish to most highly commend to everyone. ### The Third Chapter.

...power of roots. I know everything that is secret and hidden, for Wisdom, who is the mistress of all arts, teaches it to me. <note>This completes the passage from the Book of Wisdom (Wisdom of Solomon) 7:20-21, which began on the previous page.</note>

Yes, see, this is how far the ancients, through their prayer, diligence, searching, and labor, progressed in the knowledge of natural things; and because they also desired to turn away sickness and poverty—those two worst evils that plague the human race—they searched through nature to see if something might be found that would be capable of driving such evil away. And they indeed found a certain thing, which they called their stone, or the **Philosopher’s Stone** <term>Philosopher’s Stone (Stein der Weisen): In alchemy, this is a legendary substance believed to be capable of turning base metals like lead into "noble" metals like gold, and also serving as a universal medicine to cure all diseases.</term>, with which they not only drove away all diseases and transformed all base metals into gold and silver, but also performed other incredible wonders, and thus attained the crown of all arts and sciences, to the praise of God and His Wisdom, the mistress of all arts and sciences.

Having now briefly pointed out the means and ways to attain Wisdom, we feel compelled to reveal a very dangerous detour and error, in which many honest souls are ensnared and held captive. For when well-meaning souls often hear and perceive that they must seek God and His Wisdom, they do not consider that they must seek and find such a high good within themselves, in their innermost **ground of the soul** <term>Ground of the Soul (Seelengrund): A term used by German mystics (like Meister Eckhart) to describe the deepest, most sacred center of a person where the divine presence dwells.</term>, but rather
go

->By which means and ways the, etc.<-
H 5
<meta>catchword: toward (gen,)</meta>

[they] go out and seek God and His kingdom here and there, in external ceremonies and **shadow-works** <term>Shadow-works (Schattenwerke): Ritualistic or superficial religious practices that are merely "shadows" or symbols of a deeper, spiritual reality rather than the reality itself.</term>, in temples and on altars, among people and in books, etc. Thus, they seek the Living among the dead <note>A reference to Luke 24:5: "Why do you seek the living among the dead?"</note> and wish to receive life from the dead, though they can never find it there. It follows, then, that they never arrive at anything true or essential within themselves, but instead grow weary in their struggle and journey. They are ensnared and held captive by the very things that could have served them well toward life, had they used them correctly.

To escape this evil, these people must consider and believe, according to the testimony of Holy Scripture, that Christ and His kingdom—the Kingdom of God—is not to be found here or there. It does not come with external ceremonies and gestures, such that one might say: "Look, here it is!" or "There it is!"; rather, it is within them. <note>Luke 17:21</note> And it is not a matter of eating and drinking, but of righteousness, peace, and joy in the Holy Spirit <note>Romans 14:17</note>; it consists not in words, but in power. <note>1 Corinthians 4:20</note> Consequently, they must turn from the external to the internal, moving from the shadow to the body and the essence itself, which is in Christ. They must strive so that, instead of relying on an external temple, they themselves might become a temple and a dwelling place of God in the spirit.

### The Third Chapter.

toward, 1 Cor. 3:13, Eph. 2:2. Instead of the external word and teacher, one should hear Christ, the living Word, and His Spirit within oneself; instead of the external water-baptism, one should partake of the internal baptism of the Spirit and fire; and instead of the external Lord’s Supper, one should share in the internal Supper of the Lamb. And instead of external books, one should become a book oneself, in which God the Lord writes His holy, heavenly **Law of Love** with living letters of the Spirit flaming with love. <note>See Jeremiah 31:33: "I will put my law in their inward parts, and write it in their hearts."</note> 

They may indeed use external things for as long and as much as these can be helpful in some way to reach the internal, but by no means any longer, lest they become an abomination in the eyes of the Lord, or serve as a snare, a downfall, and ruin for themselves. For just as one can never arrive at a destination if one always stops on the path to stand by a signpost <note>original: "Stock" (staff or post), referring here to a directional marker or way-post</note> that points the way; so too can one never come to God, who dwells within and has His kingdom there, if one always remains clinging to the external and mistakes such **shadow-work** <term>Shadow-work (Schattenwerke): Outward rituals or symbolic practices that point toward spiritual truth but are not the divine reality itself.</term> for the essence itself. 

Therefore, take good note of this, you transgressors! Enter into your own hearts and let yourselves be taught by God and His Wisdom itself, for it is written: *They shall all be taught by God.* <note>John 6:45</note> But if this is to happen, you must learn to be still, and to rest from your own and all sinful workings,

-># Through Which Means and Ways the [etc.]<-

[...give] themselves, and with great attention listen for the voice of Wisdom, and the gentle stirrings and movements of her Spirit, and follow the same. For although Wisdom lets her voice be heard everywhere in the alleys and streets <note>A reference to Proverbs 1:20: "Wisdom crieth without; she uttereth her voice in the streets."</note>, she nevertheless remains unknown and imperceptible to you until you turn toward her within your inner self, and wait at the doorposts of her house <note>original: "an den Thür-Pfosten ihres Hauses lauret," referencing Proverbs 8:34. The word "lauret" suggests an eager, watchful waiting.</note> for what she wishes to say and teach you. And all the world must necessarily be silent before the Lord when He is to teach in His holy temple.

And these are then roughly the easiest, safest, and most proven means, and the most correct way to attain Wisdom, which the Lord has allowed us to become acquainted with in His light, and through which all the <term>Wise (Weisen): Referring here to the "Sophia-seekers" or spiritual sages throughout history who sought internal divine union.</term> who have ever lived in the world have come to Wisdom. If someone now also wishes to embark on this path and use these means, we assure him that he will not regret the effort he expends for the sake of Wisdom. For Wisdom is beautiful and imperishable, and gladly lets herself be seen by those who love her, and found by those who seek her; indeed, she meets and makes herself known to those who are fond of her. He who wishes to have her soon needs not much effort; he finds her waiting for him at his door. Yes, she herself goes about and seeks who may be worthy of her, and gladly appears to him along the way, and watches over him, so that

### The Third Chapter.

...she meets him. Wisdom 6 <note>original: "Sap. 6", referring to the Wisdom of Solomon 6:16: "For she goeth about seeking such as are worthy of her, sheweth herself favourably unto them in the ways, and meeteth them in every thought."</note>. Alone, without the fear of the Lord, faith, prayer, love, and a holy life, it is quite impossible to enter into her kinship and acquaintance. For Wisdom does not enter into a malicious soul, and does not dwell in a body subjected to sin. Wisdom 1:4 <note>original: "Sap 1, 4"</note>. But from generation to generation she gives herself into holy souls, and makes friends of God and prophets, Wisdom 7:27 <note>original: "Sap 7, 27"</note>. In doing so, she prepares them under her purifying discipline of love <note>The German "Liebes-Zucht" refers to a spiritual training or refinement rooted in divine love.</note> just as God wants them to be, so that they are capable and fit for all His good service and work; which she then also wishes to do for all those who read this.

We could certainly say much more here; but because the Kingdom of God does not consist in words, and we hold ourselves assured that Wisdom herself will tell those sincere people—who strive to follow what we have said here—the rest that we might still have to say, and will teach them everything within their inner selves; so we wish to have referred the beloved and sincere reader to that internal teacher, with the heartfelt wish that he, like that wise merchant in Matthew 13:45 <note>The original text cites "Matth. 3, 45," likely a printer's error for Matthew 13:45, the parable of the merchant seeking fine pearls.</note>, might give all that is his for this precious pearl, and receive it into his possession as his eternal property, to his greatest joy, refreshment, and delight, in time and eternity. Yes, Amen: So may it be. Amen.

### regarding Divine Wisdom.

->Melody: Now praise, my soul, etc.<- <note>original: "Nun lob mein Seel ꝛc."; this refers to the popular 16th-century Lutheran hymn "Nun lob, mein Seel, den Herren," based on Psalm 103.</note>

# A Song of Praise
## Of the Divine Wisdom.

->Melody: Now praise, my soul, etc. <note>original: "Nun lob mein Seel ꝛc."; referring to the popular 1540 Lutheran hymn by Johann Gramann, based on Psalm 103. Using a familiar melody was a common practice for new devotional poems.</note><-

O most beautiful Love!
Sophia <note>The Greek word for "Wisdom." In this mystical tradition, Wisdom is personified as a celestial bride who seeks union with the human soul.</note>, my bride!

At every hour and time;

Such great kindness,

In my great distress,

With your oil and wine.

### V.

You pulled me back
From the sinful course of corruption;
You tore the ropes asunder,
That still wished to hold me back;
You pulled me quite powerfully
With your cord of love; <note>original: "Liebes-Seil." This imagery is likely drawn from Hosea 11:4, where God leads with "cords of human kindness, with ties of love," suggesting a gentle but firm guidance.</note>
And were constantly active
For the salvation of my soul.
At one moment you made me sorrowful,
At another you filled me with great joy;
In this way, you trained me
For a considerable time.

### VI.

You have often taught me
A quite wonderful lesson, <note>original: "Lection." From the Latin *lectio*, this suggests a formal spiritual reading or educational task given by God to the soul.</note>
Yet I was so deluded,
That I did not recognize your Tone, <note>original: "Thon." In mystical thought, the "Tone" or "Sound" is the inner resonance of the Divine Word that vibrates within the believer.</note>
Which nevertheless at many times
Permeated spirit, soul, and body,
And sometimes brought me joy,
Sometimes made me fearful and anxious.
You were hidden from me,
And yet so manifest,
That every morning
I perceived you indeed.

->XII.<-

# Preface
## To the Reader.

Reader, because God has thoroughly begun to allow the spirit of medicine <term>Arzney: medicine or the healing arts</term> to work through Machaon, Podalirius, Apollo, and Hippocrates, etc. <note>These are figures from Greek mythology and history: Machaon and Podalirius were sons of Asclepius; Apollo was the god of healing; Hippocrates is known as the father of modern medicine.</note>, so that true medicine appears as if through a cloud (in which it could not yet be properly recognized) and breaks forth into the light, and also became partly manifest to mankind; so He has also checked and prevented the spirit of darkness from completely suppressing and extinguishing the <term>Light of Nature: a central concept in Paracelsian philosophy referring to the divine wisdom and knowledge imprinted by God within the natural world</term>, so that the <note>original: "Magnalia Dei"</note> great works of God, which lie hidden in <term>Arcana: secret, powerful remedies believed to have transformative properties</term>, <term>Quintessences: the purest, most concentrated essence of a substance</term>, <term>Magisteries: high-quality medicinal preparations</term>, and Elixirs, do not remain unrecognized. Therefore, God has ordered and provided through means that subsequently, through good spirits

<summary>This preface introduces the divine origin of medical knowledge, framing it as a "Light of Nature" that God reveals to humanity to push back the darkness of ignorance. It justifies the study of alchemy and medicine as a way to uncover the "great works of God" hidden within natural substances.</summary>

<keywords>medicine, Light of Nature, alchemy, divine revelation, Hippocrates, quintessence, arcana, history of science</keywords>

-># Preface to the Reader.<-

...such <term>Arcana: secret or hidden mysteries, often referring to powerful medicinal preparations</term> and <term>Mysteria: divine secrets</term> would be further instilled in man to explore: how then certain people can reach such a state that—through renewal by means of the divine spirit, or <term>Rebirth: a spiritual transformation believed to grant higher understanding</term>—they take on and receive <term>Angelic Natures</term> <note>original: "Englische Naturen." In this context, "Englisch" refers to "Engel" (angelic), not the English nation.</note> from heaven, just as the angels can. Such people are thereafter able, as those who possess a perfect understanding, to contemplate nature and its daily course more deeply than others; they can hold the pure against the impure, to separate and divide them, and to transform things to such an extent that it seems impossible to some. 

For they, as true <term>Physici: natural philosophers or physicians who study the laws of nature</term>, are able to come to the aid of nature through appropriate means, and bring nature to <term>Perfection</term> through <term>Art</term> <note>In alchemical texts, "Art" refers to the skilled practice of alchemy or "the Spagyric Art," which refines and completes what nature leaves unfinished.</note>. Therefore, all devilish and imperfect works must shrink and retreat before them, like lies against the truth and against perfection. This truth, I say, shall and must be pursued here, if one intends to come to a happy end. But if the truth is to be seized, then no one should be ashamed to seek it, no matter in what place it may be. That I have loved it [truth] highly...

It frames the "true physician" as one who separates the pure from the impure to overcome imperfect or "devilish" works.</summary>

-># Preface to the Reader.<-

...and have sought it, I am not to be blamed; for I must follow after her and seek her out, because she does not follow me, nor does she seek me. For indeed, he who wishes to reach a city must not stay sitting at home on a cushion, roasting pears, and becoming a "doctor behind the stove" <note>A derogatory German idiom for an armchair scholar who gains "wisdom" by the warmth of the fire rather than through real-world experience.</note>. No distinguished <term>Cosmographer: a scholar who maps the general features of the universe, including the heavens and the earth</term> can grow behind a table; no <term>Chiromancer: one who practices divination or medical diagnosis through the lines of the hand</term> on the floor; nor any <term>Geomancer: one who interprets signs from the earth or landscape</term> in a cellar; likewise, no true medicine can be obtained without wide-ranging investigation. God gives to a <term>Physician</term> what he desires, yet not without restlessness, toil, and labor, as He says: "You shall eat the labors of your hands, and it shall be well with you" <note>original Latin: "Labores manuum tuarum manducabis, & bene tibi erit," a reference to Psalm 128:2.</note>. Since seeing provides the truth, and whatever the sight grasps either gladdens or terrifies the heart, my toil must not be held against me—even if I travel and associate with people whom some, out of ignorance, despise. For exploring what lies hidden and buried in the <term>Limbo of the Earth: the boundary or primordial source of the terrestrial world</term> <note>original: "Limbo Terræ." In Paracelsian philosophy, this often refers to the "great mystery" or the prime matter from which things grow.</note>, as well as discovering what God has created for a true physician to use (so that benefit, rather than harm, may follow), permits no idler.

<meta>This page continues the author's argument against "stove-side doctors," contrasting his own active travels with the sedentary habits of his critics. -># Preface to the Reader.<-

Therefore, let whoever wishes sit upon the cushion; I, for my part, rejoice in my travels and in that which God and time permit me to see and to fathom.

However, for the sake of the good-hearted who intend to learn and who love the <term>Light of Nature: the innate wisdom and observable truths found within the natural world, rather than in ancient books</term>, I have also wished to write this little book. I do this so that they might possess the foundation of my <term>Medicine</term> science and leave behind the "smear-work" of the <term>Cacomedicus: a "bad doctor" (from the Greek 'kakos' for bad and the Latin 'medicus' for physician)</term>, and might also be able to offer those same people a partial answer based on my own foundation. 

I expect, however, that this book shall also be regarded as a work of fables <note>The author anticipates that established academics will dismiss his experiential knowledge as mere storytelling or fiction.</note>. For those "well-ordered fellows" <note>Referring to members of the established medical guilds and universities.</note> believe they know it all beforehand, as if <term>Doctor Donkey-Head: a satirical name for an arrogant, foolish scholar</term> already had the matter in his bag; yet he will likely leave it untouched. An <term>Alchemist</term> must be the one to understand such a little book—one to whom the burning coals do no harm, and whom the daily smoke does not make weary <note>A reference to the physical labor and resilience required in a laboratory, as opposed to the clean, idle life of a theorist.</note>. Now, let it please whoever will; I compel no one. It will not, however, pass without benefit, even though my adversaries—the so-called <term>Physicians</term>—accuse and charge me with whatever they please.

-># On the Philosopher’s Stone. <note>original: "De Lapide Philosophorum"</note><-

# On the Philosopher’s Stone.

To forge the **Philosopher’s Stone** <note>The legendary "Lapis Philosophorum," a substance capable of turning base metals into gold and creating the Elixir of Life</note> through the fire of **Vulcan** <note>The Roman god of fire and metalworking; in alchemy, this represents the heat of the furnace and the transformative power of fire</note>, which we—for sufficient reasons—call the **perfect or perpetual Balsam**: it is first necessary for us to consider how it is materially prefigured, how it becomes visible and perceptible, and also how its power or its fire emerges and makes itself known.

However, so that it may be demonstrated more understandably, we shall take an example from common fire, seeing how its power manifests and becomes visible. It happens in this way: first, through **Vulcan**, a fire is drawn out of the stone; now, such fire is capable of doing nothing unless it has matter similar to itself in which it may work: such as wood, resin, oil, and the like, which burn readily and are naturally inclined toward it. Now, when such natural fire enters into such a substance, it continues to work and does not vanish, unless it is destroyed or hindered by its **contrary** <note>original: "Contrario," referring to an opposing force like water or cold</note>, or if there is no more matter present in which it can or may **multiply**. For when one adds wood or other similar things to it, its power becomes ever greater and stronger, thus completing its work so long as nothing more is added. Regarding the nature of the wood in which the fire works and displays its action: so it **goes**

<note>original vocabulary list: Philosopher's Stone, Vulcan/The Forge, Balsam, Matter, Multiply, Contrary</note>

<summary>The author introduces the Philosopher's Stone as a "perpetual balsam" and uses the mechanics of a common wood-fire to explain how alchemical energy requires specific matter to sustain and multiply itself.</summary>
<keywords>Philosopher's Stone, alchemy, Vulcan, fire, multiplication, matter, balsam, natural philosophy</keywords>

-># On the Philosopher’s Stone.<-

is it also with the **Philosopher's Stone** <note>original: "Lapide Philosophorum"</note>, or the **perpetual Balsam** <note>original: "Balsamo perpetuo"</note> within the human body. For if it is made from proper **Matter** <note>original: "Materie"</note>, and prepared in the philosophical manner by a wise **Physician** <note>original: "Medico"</note>, and is given and used with sufficient prior consideration of all the person’s circumstances: then it renews and refreshes all the **Organs of Life** <note>original: "Organa Vitæ"</note> in such a way, as when wood is added to a fire, which enlivens the nearly dead coals and causes them to burn loud and bright once again. From this, it is actually to be concluded that very much depends upon the **Matter** of such a balsam, since it should and must have a special **Harmony** <note>original: "Harmoniam"</note> with the human body, which may accomplish its effect in such a way that the human body is secured against all evil accidents <note>In a medical context, "accidents" refers to sudden symptoms or complications of a disease</note> that any matter might cause.

Therefore, it is not only the preparation of the **Philosopher's Stone** or **Balsam** that matters, but also that, above all things, the right **Matter**, which is suitable for it, be recognized and prepared as is proper, and also used with good understanding and reason, so that such a **Medicine** <note>original: "Medicin"</note> may have the power to cleanse all impurities of the blood and other superfluities, and may grant health in place of sickness.

And for that reason, a truly pious **Physician** should have complete knowledge, and not look upon pomp, nor prescribe <note>The catchword "ordnen" from the bottom of the page indicates the text continues with "prescribe" or "order"</note> doubtful things, or those which are adverse,

For what else is it than knowingly poisoning <note>In Early New High German, "vergeben" frequently meant to poison rather than to forgive</note> a person, and yet wanting to be called "Master" despite such rogue's tricks, and even taking money and reward for it? -># On the Philosopher’s Stone.<-

...had the same illness, they themselves would not take such medicine. Therefore, it is easily seen that things are proceeding in a punishable manner, and it would be very necessary to handle the matter differently, to cast off the evil, and follow what is better. But I fear that "old dogs are hard to train" <note>The author uses the proverb "alte Hunde hart bändig zu machen seyn," suggesting that established physicians are too set in their ways to change.</note>.

However, to return to my purpose and fulfill my intention: I say that it is not advisable merely to chatter about the <term>Philosopher's Stone <note>original: "Lapide Philosophorum"</note></term> or to boast of it; rather, necessity demands that one form such a <term>Stone <note>original: "Lapidem"</note></term> from proper <term>Matter <note>original: "Materia"; the raw physical substance required for the alchemical Great Work</note></term>, prepare it as required, and use it with understanding. 

You should know, however, that several of the ancients sufficiently indicated such <term>Matter <note>original: "Materiam"</note></term> in their parabolic writings, and also revealed its preparation with figurative words, yet did not bring it to light in a completely understandable way, so that the unworthy would have no use for it, and yet it would not remain hidden from their "children" <note>In alchemical literature, "children" or "sons of art" refers to initiated students or those enlightened enough to understand the symbolic language.</note>. 

But because there are few who followed and applied themselves to it, this knowledge has faded over time, and in place of the truth, <term>Galenic fables <note>original: "Galenische Mährlein"; refers to the traditional medical teachings of Galen, which the author dismisses as mere stories compared to true chemical medicine</note></term> have taken hold. As the foundation of these fables is, so too is their effect and substance; and the longer it goes on, the worse it becomes. 

You see this with their <term>Herbals <note>original: "Herbariis"; books cataloging plants and their medicinal uses</note></term>, how they torment themselves with them, mixing Germany with Italy <note>original: "Welschland"; a historical German term for foreign, Latin-influenced lands, specifically Italy in this medical context</note>, even though Germany does not [need] <note>The sentence cuts off, but implies that German patients do not require foreign plants for healing.</note> these herbs which they call <term>overseas plants <note>original: "Transmarina"; literally "from across the sea"</note></term>...

<summary>The author argues that true medicine requires the practical preparation of the Philosopher's Stone rather than empty boasting, and criticizes the medical establishment for abandoning ancient alchemical truths in favor of ineffective foreign herbs and "Galenic fables."</summary>

-># On the Philosopher’s Stone.<-

...is required, and the perfect medicine is sufficiently available in Germany. So that the truth may not yield to lies, and the darkness of <term>Galen <note>Galen of Pergamum (129–216 AD), the ancient Greek physician whose "humoral" theories dominated European medicine for over a millennium, and whom Paracelsus vehemently opposed.</note></term> along with his <term>vermin <note>original: "Geschmeis"; a derogatory term for Galen’s followers, implying they are pests or riffraff.</note></term> does not extinguish and suppress the light of nature in medicine: it is fitting for me, Theophrastus, to speak in this little book, not as a supposed doctor, but as one who knows. I am an artist who need not be ashamed of my actions and omissions in medicine, and who—next to divine help—has proven with deeds upon many sick people what you, Galenist, would not have dared even to look upon. Tell me now, you Galenic doctor, upon what is your foundation built? Do you not <term>bridle the horse by the tail? <note>original: "Zäumest du nicht das Roß beym Arsch auf?"; a German idiom meaning to do things completely backward or incorrectly.</note></term> Have you ever cured <term>gout <note>original: "Podagram"</note></term>, or even dared to tackle <term>leprosy <note>original: "Lepram"</note></term>? Or have you ever driven out <term>dropsy <note>original: "Hydropem"; an old term for edema or swelling caused by fluid retention.</note></term>? I believe you would rightly remain silent and let me, Theophrastus, be your master.

But do you want to learn? Then learn, and behold what I write herein, and see: how the human body does not need your herbal wares, especially in those long-lasting, severe illnesses which are called <term>chronic <note>original: "Chronicos"</note></term>, and which you, out of lack of understanding, call quite <term>incurable <note>original: "incurabiles"</note></term>. For your wares are too weak for such illnesses; they cannot, through the inability of their nature, find the <term>center of the disease <note>original: "Centrum morbi"</note></term>. Nor may you accomplish anything with your pills except only to purge the <term>waste <note>original: "Excrementa"</note></term>, also often improperly driving out the good before the bad with force, which happens not without noticeable disadvantage to the sick and weak...

<summary>Paracelsus (Theophrastus) launches a fierce polemic against traditional Galenic physicians, claiming they fail to cure chronic diseases like gout and leprosy. He asserts that true medicine, based on the "light of nature," is available in Germany and targets the root of the disease rather than just purging the body.</summary>

...[natural] things cannot happen: therefore, such pill-work should rightly be avoided. If one now wishes to follow nature and use natural medicine, one must see what, among all things in medicine, is most suitable for the human body to maintain it in health until the appointed goal of death, by virtue of its power and merit. When this is considered, I have no doubt that one must recognize and say that metallic things have a very great uniformity with the human body, and that the per-

For the <term>Sulfur, Mercury, and Salt <note>Paracelsus’s "Tria Prima" or three prime principles. And when like comes to its like, and is used and applied with understanding, nature is thereby assisted. This is then a great secret in nature and medicine, and may well be called an <term>Arcanum <note>original: "Arcanum." In Paracelsian medicine, an Arcanum is a "secret" power or a highly purified medicine that acts directly on the inner life force to restore health.</note></term>. Is it any wonder, then, that excellent, unheard-of, and unexpected cures follow, which the ignorant deem impossible to heal?

But lest I stray too far, for the sake of brevity I must set down here what I intended to write in this little book, for I am willing to treat true medicine somewhat more clearly than in others.

And although it would be appropriate here to state clearly beforehand how man takes his origin from Sulfur, Mercury, and Salt, just as metals do: because I have sufficiently shown this in the <term>Paramirum <note>original: "Paramiro." Refers to Paracelsus's work *Opus Paramirum*, which details his theories on the causes of disease and the composition of the human body.</note></term>, it is unnecessary to repeat it here. The ultimate goal of alchemy, believed to be a universal medicine that can cure all diseases and prolong life.</note></term> may be recognized and prepared, etc.

Therefore, you should know that certainly nothing is so small that is meant to become something which can be without form. For everything shapes, gives birth to, increases, and destroys itself in its <term>concordance <note>original: "Concordanz." The harmonious agreement or correspondence between the macrocosm (the universe) and the microcosm (the human body).</note></term>; every end points back to its beginning; thus—

<margin>Pelican <note>A "Pelican" is a specific type of alchemical circulatory vessel with two side-arms that feed back into the main body, used for constant distillation.</note></margin>Take the immature mineral <term>Electrum</term> <note>original: "Electrum." This refers to a sublimated or "spread out" volatile spirit.</note>, and repeatedly draw off the ostrich-stomach from it; thus, the longer this continues, the more spiritual the Electrum becomes. 

But when the ostrich-stomach has become exhausted from working upon it, it is necessary to refresh it and always draw it off again. It strives to draw out and drive away "fixed" and otherwise incurable diseases; not so that it allows the diseases to act and cause harm, but so that it, as an enemy of the disease, draws its like-matter to itself, and thoroughly consumes and washes it out, just as a **soap** <note>The author uses the analogy of soap to explain the "homeopathic" principle that a purified poison can cleanse the body by attracting and removing similar toxins.</note> removes the filth from a stained cloth, with which

...it also goes away, leaving the cloth cleansed, unharmed, beautiful, and clean. Furthermore, in its virtue and power, it is as far distinguished from your **Mercury** <note>original: "Quecksilber."</note>, with which you make your salves, and from your **Precipitate** <note>original: "Præcipitat." Red mercury precipitate was a common but toxic external treatment.</note> which you use, as the heaven is from the earth. Therefore, it is and is called a blessed medicine from GOD, not revealed to everyone, which improves things far differently than the "filth-work" which **Doctor Leisentritt** displayed and filtered through his "double-hood" or **fool’s cap** <note>A pun on the *alembic* (the "cap" or "hood" of a still). The author is mocking Leisentritt’s equipment and his intelligence simultaneously.</note>.

This blessed medicine also has three times more power and virtue to act in all diseases—whatever they may be called—than all the pharmacies you have ever seen. But I did not obtain such things through idleness, sitting still, and laziness, nor did I find them in a **urine-glass** <note>original: "Harn-Glaß." Traditional physicians (Uroscopists) diagnosed all diseases by examining the color and sediment of urine, a practice alchemists often ridiculed as guesswork.</note>; rather, I had to learn it by traveling about and—as you say—by "wandering the land" <note>original: "Landfahren." Paracelsus and his followers were often mocked as vagabonds because they traveled to learn from miners, blacksmiths, and folk healers rather than staying in universities.</note> and with much diligence and research, so that I might *know*, and not merely *suppose*. But you suck your medicine out of an old bench-cushion, and from...

Therefore, practice yourself, and let it be a serious matter to you, for this will bring you honor. ...grasp and understand. Thus its virtue is also infinite and unspeakable, so that God is recognized within it.

<margin>3</margin>You should know, however, that no dissolution occurs in your **Electrum** <term>Electrum: In alchemical texts, this often refers to a specific "magical" alloy of the seven planetary metals, or a highly purified metallic starting material.</term> unless it has completely run through the circle of the **seven spheres** <term>Seven Spheres: This refers to the seven planetary heavens (Moon, Mercury, Venus, Sun, Mars, Jupiter, Saturn), symbolizing the need for the matter to undergo a complete cycle of refinement or "distillation" corresponding to these cosmic forces.</term> three times; for such a number belongs to it and it must complete it. Therefore, God gives it to whom He wills; it cannot be forced by violence, but God alone wishes to have the glory in this; His name be praised in eternity, Amen.

# Prologue

...were excellent philosophers, as well as **Spagyrists** <term>Spagyrist: A term coined by Paracelsus for an alchemist who uses the "art of separation" to extract medicinal virtues from raw matter.</term> and **Astronomers**. That they were so is testified even by your own adversaries, who remain your idols and patrons to this very hour, O you Sophist! And even if your "authentic fathers" and falsely appointed saints made no mention of it, the ancient **Emerald Tablet** <note>original: "smaragdische Tafel." A foundational Hermetic text said to contain the secrets of the universe and the "Great Work" of alchemy.</note> still demonstrates more art and experience in philosophy, medicine, magic, and the like, than will ever be learned by you and your lot.

As for what their treasures and goods were—if you do not understand it from what has already been said—tell me this: why has no potentate been able to subject the **Egyptians** to his power? Tell me also: why did the Emperor **Diocletian** <note>Diocletian (r. 284–305 AD) is said to have ordered the burning of Egyptian alchemical texts to prevent them from making gold and funding revolts against Rome.</note> have all the **Spagyric** books that were brought to him burned? If they had possessed or known nothing, they would surely have long since been forced to unjustly bear the yoke that will yet fall upon the neck of you, Sophist, and those like you.

Now, in this midnight age, <note>original: "mittern Sæculo." Paracelsus often used this term to describe his own era as a time of hidden light or a turning point in history.</note> the monarchy of all arts has finally [come] to me, **Theophrastus**, Prince of Philosophy and Medicine—

# Prologue

L 3

Instead, it will begin to flourish in the year 1558, and the practice that follows it will prove itself with incredible signs and miraculous deeds, so that even craftsmen and the common people will understand how the art of **Theophrastus** <note>Theophrastus was the name Paracelsus preferred to use for himself, referencing the Greek philosopher and successor to Aristotle.</note> stands firm against the botchery of the **Sophists** <note>Sophist: Here used as a derogatory term for university-educated physicians who Paracelsus believed relied on empty rhetoric and ancient texts rather than clinical experience.</note>, which, because of its own incompetence, requires the protection and confirmation of Papal and Imperial privileges.

These false slanders you have devised against me have often vexed many counts and lords, as well as the Imperial cities and the general knighthood. # Prologue

...excellent philosophers they were, and what **Spagyrists** <term>Spagyrist: A practitioner of spagyric medicine, which involves the alchemical separation and recombination of plant or mineral components to create potent remedies.</term> and **astronomers**! For that they were such, even your own adversaries testify—those who, oh you **Sophist** <note>Sophist: Here used as a derogatory term for university-educated physicians who Paracelsus believed relied on empty rhetoric and ancient texts rather than clinical experience.</note>, remain your idols and patrons to this very hour. And even if no mention of this were made by your "authentic" fathers or your falsely appointed saints, the ancient **Emerald Tablet** <note>original: *smaragdische Tafel*; the *Tabula Smaragdina*, a foundational Hermetic text said to contain the secrets of the universe and the transformation of matter.</note> still demonstrates more art and experience in philosophy, medicine, magic, and the like than will ever be learned by you and your crowd.

Now, as for what their treasures and goods were—if you do not understand it from what preceded—tell me: why was no potentate able to subject the **Egyptians** to his power? Tell me also, why did the Emperor **Diocletian** <note>Diocletian (r. 284–305 AD) reportedly ordered the burning of Egyptian alchemical texts to prevent the Egyptians from making gold and using that wealth to fund rebellions.</note> have all the **Spagyric books** that fell into his hands burned? If they had possessed or known nothing, they would surely have long ago and unjustly had to bear the yoke that will yet fall upon the necks of you, Sophist, and those of your kind.

Now, in this middle century, the monarchy of all arts has finally [come] to me, **Theophrastus**, Prince of Philosophy and Medicine...

# Prologue

Instead, it will begin to flourish in the year 1558, and the practice that follows will prove itself with incredible signs and miraculous deeds. These slanders you have falsely devised against me have often vexed many counts and lords, along with the Imperial cities and the general knighthood. # Prologue

<meta>catchword: Of</meta>

**Friuli** <note>original: *Fryaul*; a region in northeastern Italy where Paracelsus traveled and practiced medicine.</note> is a jewel in the hospital, which neither you, the Roman Lion <note>A reference to the Pope or the power of the Roman Catholic Church.</note>, nor German Charles <note>Emperor Charles V (1500–1558).</note> can afford with all your power. Even though the **Signatory Star** <term>Signatory Star: *Signat-Stern*; in Paracelsian philosophy, the celestial influence that imprints or "signs" a physical object with its hidden internal virtues and healing properties.</term> has fallen into the secret of your names, it is recognized by none except the sons of the divine **Spagyric Art** <term>Spagyric Art: A term coined by Paracelsus for alchemy, specifically the process of "separating and recombining" substances to create potent medicines.</term>.

Therefore, so that you, lousy **Sophist** <note>Paracelsus used "Sophist" as a derogatory term for traditional university doctors who relied on ancient books rather than practical experience.</note>, do not mistake the **Monarch of Arcana** <note>Paracelsus refers to himself as the supreme master of *Arcana*, the hidden healing secrets of nature.</note> for an ignorant fool and a wasteful spendthrift, I will—for the enlightenment and better progress of the present **era** <note>original: *Sæculo*; Latin for "age" or "century."</note>—detail the preparation and virtue of the **Tincture of the Physicians** <note>original: *Tinctura Physicorum*; a legendary alchemical medicine believed to be the ultimate restorative and a key to transmuting both metals and health.</note> in this little book. This is so that the lovers of truth may be advanced to wealth and honor, while the despisers of the upright arts become beggars and a cover for shame. Following this, the final world of grace will shine forth and visibly exalt itself in the gifts of the true spirit; such understanding and wisdom will not have been heard of since the beginning of the world. Meanwhile, vice shall not suppress the pious, and the wealth of such people will not be perceived by the many.

# On the Tincture of the Physicians, Ph.
## By Theophrastus Bombast.

### Chapter I.

I, **Philippus Theophrastus Bombast**, declare that through the revelation of divine grace, many different paths toward the **Tincture of the Physicians** <term>Tincture of the Physicians: *Tinctura Physicorum*; a legendary alchemical substance capable of perfecting health and transmuting base matter into gold.</term> have been sought, all of which ultimately desired to reach a single goal and end. Therefore, **Hermes Trismegistus** <note>The legendary Hellenistic figure to whom the foundational texts of alchemy and Hermeticism are attributed.</note> the Egyptian went to work according to his own mind; **Orus** <note>Likely Horus, an Egyptian deity often cited in early alchemical lineages as a source of ancient wisdom.</note> the Greek undertook the same process; **Hali** <note>An Arabic master, likely referring to the astrologer and alchemist Haly Abenragel.</note>, an Arabian master, remained within his own order; the German **Albertus** <note>Albertus Magnus (c. 1200–1280), the medieval scholar and friar rumored to have discovered the Philosopher's Stone.</note> also followed the "long path." Thus, each of these followed his own intellect, yet in the end, they all arrived at one goal and purpose: namely, long life—which is most highly desired by the philosophers—and the honorable maintenance of that life in this **vale of tears** <note>original: *Jammerthal*; a common historical term for the suffering and hardships of earthly existence.</note>.

But now, the divine gift has reached me, **Philippus Theophrastus Bombast**, the **Monarch of Arcana** <note>Paracelsus’s title for himself as the supreme master of nature’s hidden healing secrets.</note>. From this point forward, everyone who dares to undertake the highest work of **Physics** <term>Physics: *Physic*; in the 16th century, this referred broadly to the study of natural laws and the practice of medicine.</term> will have to walk in my footsteps and follow me—whether you be Italian, Polish, French, or German, or whoever you may be or shall become. All philosophers, astronomers, and **spagyrists** <term>Spagyrists: *Spagirici*; practitioners of the alchemical art of "separating and joining" to create potent medicines.</term> must follow after me; I will [show] you **Alchemists** and **Doctors**...

<summary>Paracelsus establishes his supreme authority over the alchemical tradition, claiming that while past masters like Hermes and Albertus Magnus reached the same goal through various paths, he has now received the ultimate divine revelation of the Arcanum.</summary>

...authors, through my endured labor, I shall reveal the new birth. I will teach you the Tincture, the Secret <note>original: *Arcanum*; a hidden healing power or spiritual substance.</note>, or the Fifth Essence <note>original: *quintum esse*; the quintessence or purest part of a substance, believed to be the most potent medicinal form.</note>, in which all hidden secrets, the foundation, and the work reside. For each man should believe another only as much as he has experienced through fire; and whatever further idle chatter one might bring forth, whether in Spagyrics <term>Spagyrics: The alchemical practice of separating, purifying, and recombining the medicinal parts of plants and minerals to create potent remedies.</term> or Medicine, you shall give it no faith or credit whatsoever, unless it has been proven through fire, which teaches how to separate the false from the true.

From this light, we shall teach and instruct regarding all matters undertaken—not from our own fantasy as others have done, who before me have been turned into fools by their own subtle heads. Therefore, finally, upon my foundation, many a peasant shall become a nobleman; whereas, conversely, many a nobleman shall become a peasant through that presumed art, if he carries mountains of gold in his head before he has even plunged his hands into the coals.

# Chapter II. On the Tincture of the Physicians, etc.

[...which is the] Almighty's revelation, and may be for the contemplation of all people on earth; and that is the Lily of Medicine and Alchemy, which the philosophers have sought so vehemently and rigorously, but due to a lack of complete knowledge and perfect preparation, they did not bring it to its fullest conclusion. For through their investigation and experience, the beginning of the Tincture was given to them, and the true foundation, which my fellow-kin should follow, is rightfully left to me, so that no one else may cast their masks <note>original: *Larven*; here referring to deceptions, false appearances, or "ghosts" of failed theories that obscure the truth.</note> into our undertaking. 

I will rightfully, after my long experience and practical testing, improve the **spagyric art** <term>Spagyrics: The alchemical process of separating substances into their elemental components (purifying them) and then recombining them to create a more potent medical "essence."</term> and separate the false or erroneous from the good. It is my place, after long searching, to improve and change these things. If, however, I had found the tests of the ancients to be more excellent than my own, I would have spared much toil and labor, which I have endured for the benefit, praise, and honor of all pious alchemists. 

Since the Subject <note>original: *Subjectum*; the raw material or primary matter required for the alchemical work.</note> of the Tincture has now been sufficiently explained—as far as one brother may show loyalty to another—I will further proceed to its preparation. Alongside showing the experience of the first Age <note>original: *Sæculi*; Paracelsus suggests that different eras have different levels of medical revelation.</note>, I will also add my own discovery, which will eventually be embraced by the Age of Grace <note>original: *Sæculum gratiæ*; a theological and alchemical concept of a future time when divine secrets and perfect health would be revealed to humanity.</note>. You, Sophist <note>Sophist: A term of derision used by Paracelsus for university-trained doctors who relied on rhetoric and ancient books rather than hands-on laboratory experience and the "Light of Nature."</note>, may make of yourself and your philosophical patriarchs whatever you wish.

# Chapter III. On the Tincture of the Physicians, etc. <note>original: *De Tinctura Physicorum*</note>
<meta>catchword: their</meta>

### Chapter III.

The ancient philosophers rotted <note>original: *gefaulet*; in alchemy, this refers to "putrefaction," the process of breaking down a substance through gentle, moist heat to prepare it for transformation.</note> the **Lily** <term>Lily: A symbolic term in Paracelsian alchemy often referring to the purified white stage of the work or a specific medicinal essence.</term> for a "Philosophical Month" <note>Usually a period of forty days.</note>, and afterward drew the moist spirits from it until the dry spirits rose up. But they soaked the moist spirits back into the **dead head** <term>Caput mortuum: The "dead head" or the solid dregs and useless residue remaining at the bottom of a flask after the distillation process.</term> and drove them upward again and again, until all the dry spirits were raised. After this, they reunited the purified moist and dry spirits with one another for a third or fourth time using the **Pelican** <term>Pelican: A specialized alchemical vessel used for "circulation." It has two side-arms that lead from the neck back into the body, allowing distilled vapors to condense and flow back into the original liquid, effectively "feeding" itself.</term>, until the entire **Lily** lay dry at the bottom.

Although the first experience of the **Tincture** <note>The "Tincture" is the legendary substance—often called the Philosopher's Stone—capable of healing the body or "tinging" base metals into gold.</note> provided this process before the **fixation** <note>original: *Figirung*; the process of making a volatile substance stable and resistant to heat.</note>, and our ancestors often achieved mastery of their desires through it, they would have had a much closer path to the **Red Lion’s** treasury <note>The "Red Lion" is a symbol for the final, most potent stage of the alchemical work, often associated with gold or the ultimate medicine.</note> if they had learned the **Concordance of Astronomy with Alchemy**, as I have indicated in the *Apocalypse of Hermes* <note>original: *Apocalypsi Hermetis*; a text attributed to Paracelsus regarding the revelation of hidden wisdom.</note>. But because every day has its own trouble—as our Savior announces to all Christian believers for their comfort—bitter labor was reserved for the **spagyrists** <term>Spagyrist: An alchemist who practices the "spagyric art" of separating substances into their pure components and then recombining them for medicinal use.</term> before my time. Now, in this final age, their work will be enlightened through the help of the **floating spirit** <note>original: *schwebender Geist*; likely referring to a highly refined, volatile spirit or universal solvent.</note> and my own practice and theory, for all those who remain constant and patient in their labors. For I have experienced nature’s property, essence, and kind, and I know both its assembly and, in turn, its

<summary>Paracelsus describes the traditional "putrefaction" and distillation methods of the ancients but claims his new system, linking astronomy to alchemy, offers a more efficient path to the Great Work.</summary>

# Chapter III. On the Tincture of the Physicians, etc. <note>original: *De Tinctura Physicorum*</note>

### Chapter III.

...their dissolution, which is the highest and greatest achievement for a **Physician** <term>Physico/Physician: In this context, it refers to a natural philosopher or alchemist who understands the hidden forces of nature, rather than a modern medical doctor.</term>, and which has never been recognized or put into practice by the **Sophists** <term>Sophists: A derogatory term used by Paracelsus to describe traditional scholars who argue with logic rather than working with the actual "labors" of nature.</term> until now.

Therefore, just as the first experience of the **Tincture** <note>The legendary "Philosopher's Stone" or universal medicine.</note> was granted in the first **Century** <note>original: *Sæculo*</note>, the **Spagyrics** <term>Spagyrics: Alchemists who practice the art of separating, purifying, and recombining natural substances.</term> made two things out of one simple substance. Afterward, because this discovery was lost for half a century, their successors finally arrived at two names for that same simple substance through diligent research; they called it by the single word **Lily** <term>Lily: A symbolic name for the purified matter or the "Subject" (starting material) of the alchemical work.</term>, as the **Subject** <note>original: *Subjectum*</note> of the Tincture. 

Finally, these followers of Nature allowed this matter to rot <note>original: *gefaulet*; refers to "putrefaction," a stage where the substance is broken down by gentle heat.</note> in its own time, just like a seed in the earth, because before this "breaking" or decomposition, nothing can grow from it, and no **Arcanum** <term>Arcanum: The "secret" or most potent medicinal essence hidden within a physical substance.</term> can emerge. Afterward, they carefully drew the moist spirits away from the matter until they finally, with the greater force of fire, also raised the dry spirits. In this way, like a farmer, they followed the ripening of the fruit according to the seasons, as one thing is accustomed to rise and fall after another. Finally, just as summer appears after spring, they made the moist and dry spirits corporeal <note>meaning they gave the spirits a physical, solid form.</note> and brought the mastery of the Tincture so far that it entered into its **Ether** <note>original: *Aeher*; refers to a highly refined, celestial, or volatile state of the substance.</note> and was ready for ripening.
