# Chapter IV. On the Tincture of the Physicians, etc. <note>original: *De Tinctura Physicorum*</note>

### Chapter IV.

Now, the ancient **Spagyrics** <term>Spagyrics: Alchemists who practice the art of separating, purifying, and recombining natural substances to create medicines.</term> would not have required this tedious labor and annoying repetition if they had learned from my school and begun their work there; they would have just as easily obtained their desire, and all with less cost and effort. But now, because I, **Theophrastus** <note>The author, Paracelsus, whose full name was Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim.</note>, the **Monarch of Secrets** <note>original: *Arcanen Monarch*; refers to a supreme master of hidden natural mysteries.</note>, have arrived, the time of discovery is at hand—a time which was withheld from all Spagyrics before me. Therefore, I say to you: take nothing more than the rose-colored blood of the **Lion** <term>Lion: A symbolic name for the "fixed" or solid part of the matter, often associated with gold or antimony.</term> and the white gluten of the **Eagle** <term>Eagle: A symbolic name for the "volatile" or spirituous part of the matter, often associated with mercury or sal ammoniac.</term>. After you have joined these together, congeal them according to the old process; then you will have the **Tincture of the Physicians** <note>original: *Tincturam Physicorum*; the legendary universal medicine or "Philosopher’s Stone."</note>, which many have pursued but few have found.

You, **Sophist** <term>Sophist: Paracelsus’s term for traditional university scholars who relied on logic and old books rather than practical laboratory work.</term>, let this be a **Mystery** of Nature to me, a **Great Work of God** <note>original: *Magnale Dei*</note>, and a treasure in this valley of misery: that a lowly thing of external appearance can be changed into another, far nobler thing that did not exist before. Let this now be a wonder and a sign from the **Spagyrist** <note>original: *Spagiro*</note>: that through his art of preparation, he destroys the lowly external body and creates from it another body and essence—one that is indeed the noblest and most precious. Can you do anything like this using the **Aristotelian light** <note>The traditional philosophical and logical framework taught in universities, which Paracelsus viewed as "dim" compared to the "light of nature."</note> or **Serapion's rules** <term>Serapion: Refers to an ancient medical authority whose texts were standard curriculum, but whom Paracelsus considered outdated.</term>? If...

# Chapter IV. On the Tincture of the Physicians, etc. <note>original: *De Tinctura Physicorum*</note>

Step forward with your proof, and perform your duty as befits an honor-loving master. But if you know and can do nothing, why do you denounce me as an irrational **Swiss-cow** <note>original: *Schweiker-Kuhe*; a derogatory term likely mocking Paracelsus’s Swiss origins by comparing him to a common dairy cow.</note> and a wandering charlatan?

**Art** (if you are not yet so deeply grounded and established in the **Light of Grace** <note>The "Lumen Gratiae," a Paracelsian concept referring to divine enlightenment that allows a physician to see the hidden secrets of nature.</note>), Art, I say, is another nature and a special world, as experience testifies against you and your idols. Therefore, the Alchemist sometimes puts several simple things together, and after he has destroyed them according to his necessity, he prepares something else from them; for in this way, one single thing is often made out of many, which afterward achieves more than Nature was previously capable of on her own. This has been sufficiently proven in **Gastein**, making **Copper** from **Saturn** <term>Saturn: The alchemical name for Lead.</term>; in **Carinthia**, **Silver** <term>Luna: The alchemical name for Silver.</term> from **Mercury** <term>Mercury: Quicksilver.</term>; and in **Hungary**, **Gold** from **Silver**.

I will remain silent about other transformations of natural things, well enough known to the wise, which show themselves to be even stranger than those in all of **Ovid’s** **Metamorphoses** <note>The classical work by the Roman poet Ovid detailing mythical transformations; Paracelsus argues that alchemical reality is more wondrous than Roman myth.</note>. Therefore, if you understand me rightly, seek your **Lion** <note>The "fixed" or solid substance, often associated with gold or antimony.</note> in the East <note>original: *Aufgang*; literally the "rising" of the sun.</note>, and your **Eagle** <note>The "volatile" or spiritual substance, often associated with mercury.</note> toward the South <note>original: *Mittag*; literally "midday," the direction of the sun at its zenith.</note> for our intended work; for you will find no better tools than what **Hungary** and **Istria** <note>Regions famous for their mines and mineral wealth in the 16th century.</note> can provide.

But if you desire to bring it from **Unity** through **Duality** into **Trinity** <note>A common alchemical progression referring to the purification of the "One" matter into its constituent "Three Primes" (Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury) before recombination.</note> with a balanced alternation of each, then you must direct your path toward the South, and there in **Cyprus** <note>The island of Cyprus was historically associated with copper and the goddess Venus.</note> you will find all your

# Chapter V. On the Tincture of the Physicians, etc. <note>original: *De Tinctura Physicorum*</note>

...[of any] request <note>The OCR begins mid-word with "ner Bitt," likely completing "einer Bitt" (a request).</note> be granted, of which nothing more is to be said, as has just now been reported. For of these secrets, which produce **transformations** <note>original: *Transformationes*</note>, there are even more, although they are known to few. And even if they are revealed to someone by God, the fame of the Art does not break forth immediately; rather, the Almighty also grants him the understanding to withhold it from others until the coming of **Elias the Artist** <term>Elias the Artist: A messianic figure in alchemical lore predicted by Paracelsus; he was expected to appear in the "last days" to reveal all the secrets of nature and alchemy that were previously hidden.</term>, when that which is hidden shall be made manifest. 

Thus you see clearly—though it is not fitting to speak of it, and it might perhaps be taken as a mockery—that within the fire of **Sulfur** lies a great and wondrous **Stone-Tincture** <note>The essential power or "soul" of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, which can elevate gemstones higher than nature can, and brings them forth from itself. But I will leave aside these **gradations** <term>Gradations: The alchemical processes used to improve the color, purity, or "grade" of a metal or gemstone.</term> of metals and stones, since I have written enough about them in **The Secrets of Secrets** <note>original: *Arcanis Arcanorum*</note>, in the **Book on the Vexations of Alchemists** <note>original: *Libro de Vexationibus Alchimistarum*</note>, and in other books; and I will fully conclude the process of our ancestors, **On the Tincture of the Physicians** <note>original: *de Tinctura Physicorum*</note>, as I have begun.

### Chapter V.

Finally, the ancient **Spagyrics** <term>Spagyrics: A term coined by Paracelsus for alchemists who produce medicines by separating (extracting) and recombining natural substances.</term> fixed the **pelicanized** <note>Refers to "pelicanizing," a method of circulatory distillation using a vessel called a "pelican," where the liquid continuously evaporates and flows back down to its base.</note> dry **Lily** <term>Lily: An alchemical code name for the purified material, often representing the state of the "White Tincture."</term> with a proper increase of fire, for so long until it turned from blackness—after the alternation of all colors—to blood-red, and [took on] a **salamandrine nature** <note>original: *Salamandrische Art*; refers to the mythical salamander that lives in fire; in alchemy, this describes a substance that has become fire-proof and perfected by heat.</note>

# Chapter V. On the Tincture of the Natural Philosophers, etc. <note>original: *De Tinctura Physicorum*</note>

...[the] nature of the Art itself. <note>This sentence completes a thought from the previous page regarding the character of the alchemical process.</note> Now, in this work, they have proceeded correctly, and from this point forward, every person who desires to obtain this Pearl <note>A common metaphor for the Philosopher's Stone or the ultimate alchemical achievement.</note> shall proceed in the same manner. However, that I might set this before you more clearly in writing is difficult for me to do; instead, you must go to school with the **Alchemists**, so that you may know how to correctly maintain the **degrees of the fire** <term>Degrees of Fire: The four stages of heat used in alchemical operations, ranging from gentle warmth (like a brooding hen) to intense heat.</term> and how to change your vessels. Then you will see, as soon as the **Lily** <term>Lily: An alchemical symbol for the purified white stage of the work, often associated with the "Tincture."</term> is warmed in the **physical egg** <term>Physical Egg: Also called the "Philosophical Egg," a hermetically sealed glass vessel shaped like an egg where the Great Work takes place.</term>, that through a strange manifestation it will become blacker than a raven, then in time whiter than a swan, and finally, passing through yellow, it will become redder than Indian saffron.

Seek, seek, says the highest **Alchemist** <term>Spagyrus: A term coined or popularized by Paracelsus for a chemist who separates and recombines substances; here he applies the title to the Divine.</term>, and you shall find; knock, and it shall be opened unto you. For it would be a sin and a shame to smear the mash into the mouth of such a bad bird and not let him fly for it himself, as I and all others before me have had to do. Therefore, follow the true Art, and you shall receive perfect knowledge within it. Otherwise, there is nothing more to be added here or explained beyond what I have said before. Let your **Pharisaic schools** <note>A derogatory reference to traditional academic and religious authorities who Paracelsus believed taught empty, unproven theories.</note> teach you what they will from their unproven and dilapidated foundation—which has reached neither its goal nor its end, nor has it been proven in the **light of nature** <term>Light of Nature: Paracelsus's concept of an inner, divine wisdom that allows one to understand the secrets of the natural world.</term>.

# Chapter V. On the Tincture of the Natural Philosophers, etc. <note>original: *De Tinctura Physicorum*</note>

**M**

When you have finally learned and experienced the handiwork of the **Alchemists** to the highest degree with the greatest diligence, then there is nothing so subtle or hard in the things of nature that cannot be revealed to you through the help of this art. For nature yields nothing for use that is finished to its highest perfection in its own place, as is seen here in our union of qualities; rather, man must bring it to that point for which it was ordained by nature through **spagyric** <term>Spagyric: A term derived from the Greek words for "to separate" and "to unite." It refers to the alchemical process of purifying a substance by breaking it down into its core elements and then recombining them in a more potent form.</term> preparation. Let this be said enough regarding the ancient process and my improvement of the **Physical Tincture** <note>original: *Physischen Tinctur*; the "tincture" refers to the ultimate alchemical medicine or "Stone" that can transform both health and metal.</note>, as far as the preparation is concerned.

Furthermore, since we now hold the **Egyptian Treasure** <note>A metaphorical reference to the secret knowledge of alchemy, which was traditionally believed to have originated in ancient Egypt with figures like Hermes Trismegistus.</note> in our hands, we shall henceforth see how we ought to make use of and apply the same, which now offers itself to us in a twofold way from this spagyric secret: the first, how it may be employed for the **renewal of the human body**; the other, how it should be used for the **transmutation of metals**. Since I, **Theophrastus** <term>Theophrastus: The author refers to himself by his given name, Theophrastus von Hohenheim, though he is better known to history as Paracelsus.</term>, have experienced both of these in many ways, I will describe them according to the signs of their effect and present them as I have found them best in my testing.

### Chapter VI.

If the **Physical Tincture** <note>original: *Physische Tinctur*; the supreme alchemical substance or "Stone" used for transformation.</note> is to be used for the transformation of metals, then first one part of it must be cast upon a thousand parts of gold standing in flux <note>i.e., in a molten state</note>; only then is the medicine prepared to drive out the **leprosy of metals** <term>Leprosy of metals: An alchemical metaphor for the impurities and "baseness" found in non-noble metals like iron or lead, which were seen as "diseased" versions of gold.</term> and to change them into perfect ones. Let it be a particularly great wonder in the **light of nature** <note>A term for the innate knowledge of the natural world and its secrets, granted by God through observation and experience.</note> that through the work of the **spagyrist** <term>Spagyrist: An alchemist who practices the art of "spagyria"—the separation and recombination of natural substances to achieve higher purity and potency.</term> a metal might lose its form and take on another: a fact which made a fool of the groundless **Aristotle** in his philosophy.

For when the peasants in **Hungary**, when they place iron for a time in the **Zipser wells** <note>Referring to the Spiš (Zips) region in modern-day Slovakia. The waters there were naturally rich in copper sulfate, which caused a chemical displacement reaction with iron.</note>, it is eaten away into a rust; when this is passed through the smelting furnace, it becomes from that hour a pure copper, which can never be returned to iron. Likewise, at **Kuttenberg** <note>Kutná Hora, a famous medieval mining town in Bohemia, modern-day Czech Republic.</note> they pour a **vitriol-lye** <term>Vitriol-lye: Acidic mine water (Kislaugen) containing dissolved copper salts. When iron is submerged in it, copper precipitates onto the iron's surface, appearing as a magical transformation.</term> in which iron instantly becomes good copper—highly refined and more malleable under the hammer than natural copper.

There are even more of these things, better known to common folk than to the **Sophists** <note>Here, "Sophists" refers to academic scholars who rely on ancient texts rather than practical experimentation and the observation of nature.</note>, which change the nature of metals into another. But these are kept secret, partly because of the great contempt shown by the ignorant, and partly out of the zealous hearts of the pious artists. Therefore, in **Istria** I have many times [made] **Venus** <note>original: *Venerem*; the alchemical name and planetary correspondence for copper.</note> over the

...brought from twenty-four up to twenty-eight degrees: so that the gold color may rise no higher, remaining constant in **Antimony** <note>A metalloid used in the purification of gold; it was believed to "devour" impurities.</note> and **Quartation** <term>Quartation: A refining process where gold is alloyed with three parts silver (making the gold one-quarter of the mass) so that nitric acid can then dissolve the silver, leaving pure gold behind.</term>, and all has been accomplished that is to be accomplished.

Although the ancient artists were very eager for this secret and sought it with the most industrious diligence, yet few of them were able to bring it to a conclusion through a perfect preparation. These chickens subsequently lost their feathers and, as I have seen myself, grew them back again. Thus, through this mishandling and the ignorance of the **Vulcanic artists** <note>Alchemists who work with fire (named after Vulcan, the Roman god of the forge).</note>, the art of **Transmutation** <note>The changing of one substance into another, specifically base metals into gold.</note> shifted from **Alchemy** into **Medicine** <note>original: *Arzney*.</note>. For because they did not know how to use the Tincture according to their desires [for metallic wealth], they turned it toward the renewal of man, as will clearly follow in its own chapter.

->### Chapter VII. On the Tincture of the Natural Philosophers, etc. <note>original: *Caput VII. De Tinctura Physicorum, &c.*</note><-

### Chapter VII.

This is the Tincture by which some of the first natural philosophers <note>original: *Physicis*; in this context, it refers to those who study the secrets of nature and medicine.</note> in Egypt, as well as in this present time, lived for one hundred and fifty years. The lives of many have also been prolonged and extended over several centuries <note>original: *Sæcula*</note>, as histories publicly demonstrate, even if such a thing seems credible to no one. For its power is so miraculous that it brings the body to a higher state than its innate constitution <note>original: *Complexion*; the specific balance of the four humors (blood, phlegm, black bile, yellow bile) believed to determine health and temperament.</note> had shown him, and maintains it steadfastly at that same degree, so that it remains free from all diseases; and although he may be burdened with age, he nevertheless appears as if placed in his former youth. Thus the Physician’s Tincture is a Universal <note>A "Universal" medicine was the ultimate goal of alchemy: a single substance capable of curing every possible ailment and purifying both metals and the human body.</note>, which, like an invisible fire, consumes all diseases, by whatever name they may be called. Its dose is very small, but the effect is very powerful. Therefore, I have cured leprosy <note>original: *Aussatz*</note>, the French sickness <term>The French Sickness: A common historical name for syphilis, which was spreading rapidly across Europe in the 16th century.</term>, dropsy <note>original: *Wassersucht*; an old term for edema or swelling caused by fluid retention.</note>, colic, the falling sickness <note>original: *hinfallende Sucht*; epilepsy.</note>, stroke, gout <note>original: *Podagra*</note>, likewise "the wolf" <note>original: *Wolf*; typically referring to lupus or other corrosive skin ulcers.</note>, cancer, fistula, sirens <note>original: *Siren*; a term used in Paracelsian medicine for various types of creeping or spreading skin conditions.</note>, and all kinds of internal defects, more than one man can remember; for which Germany, England, France, Italy <note>original: *Welschland*</note>, Poland, Bohemia, and the whole Empire will give me sufficient testimony.

## Concerning the Revelation of the Hidden Spirit of Nature.

Hermes, Plato, Aristotle, and the other <term>philosophers</term> <note>In this context, "philosophers" refers specifically to "natural philosophers" or alchemists who sought to understand the hidden laws of the physical world.</note>, who flourished at various times and were the inventors of the arts, particularly those who diligently observed the powers of the lower creatures <note>This refers to the "Great Chain of Being," specifically the animal, vegetable, and mineral kingdoms.</note>, sought with great desire to find whether anything could be found among all created things that might preserve the human body from decay and maintain it in a constant state of life. 

To this quest, it was answered: there is nothing that can release the mortal body from death itself, but there is indeed one thing that might take away <term>corruption</term> <note>In alchemy, "corruption" refers to the process of aging, disease, and physical breakdown.</note>, renew youth, and extend this short life, just as it was for the ancient <term>patriarchs</term> <note>Biblical figures like Methuselah who were said to have lived for hundreds of years.</note>. For death was imposed as a punishment upon our first ancestors, Adam and Eve, and it cannot be separated from their descendants. Therefore, the aforementioned philosophers and others

<summary>This page introduces an alchemical text attributed to Paracelsus, framed as a "revelation" regarding the quest for a substance to combat physical decay and prolong human life. It establishes a lineage of wisdom connecting ancient Greek philosophers to the alchemical tradition's search for the "Spirit of Nature."</summary>

<keywords>alchemy, Paracelsus, Hermes Trismegistus, elixir of life, Spirit of Nature, physical decay, longevity, natural philosophy</keywords>

header: Of the Revelation of [the...]
<meta>catchword: wor=</meta>

...more, they sought that one thing above all else with great labor, and have found that this same thing—which then preserves the human body from its decay and prolongs life—behaves in all its properties toward the other elements as the heavens do. From this they understood that the heavens are a being above the four elements, and thus above the four <term>qualities</term> <note>The four primary qualities in ancient science: hot, cold, moist, and dry.</note>; therefore, the heavens, when compared to the other elements, are considered the **fifth essence** <note>original: "fünfte Wesen." This is the Quintessence or Aether, a substance thought to be incorruptible and divine, unlike the four earthly elements of earth, water, air, and fire.</note>, for they are indestructible, unchangeable, and suffer no outside interference.

Thus is that one thing, which works to drive away those forces that act against our body, and it has been called by those **philosophers** by the name mentioned above. It is neither hot nor dry like the earth, but is a skillful and perfect balance of all elements, a true blending of the necessary powers, a special union of spiritual virtues, an invisible unification of body and soul—the purest and noblest essence of an indestructible body, artificially extracted, which cannot be destroyed or attacked by the elements. With this [essence], **Aristotle** prepared an apple that prolonged his life by its scent alone, when, due to his great age, he could neither eat nor drink for fifteen days before his death. <note>This refers to a popular medieval legend that Aristotle used alchemical scents to sustain his life in his final days.</note>

This spiritual essence and singular thing was revealed to **Adam** from above, and was particularly desired by the holy fathers...

It is a guide to all good, ensuring that one can neither speak nor think evil; it gives to man what he desires in his heart—granting the pious temporal honor and long life, but to the wicked who misuse it, eternal punishment. It is sought by many, but found by few; it is observed from afar, yet taken from nearby; for it is found in every thing, in every place, and at every time. 

It possesses the powers and effects of all creatures, exists equally in all elements, and whatever property any and every thing has, that is found most powerfully in this single thing. From its powers, **Adam** and the other patriarchs <note>In alchemical lore, biblical figures like Adam and the Patriarchs were often believed to have possessed the Philosopher's Stone, explaining their legendary longevity.</note> possessed the health of their bodies and long life; some among them flourished in great wealth. For if everyone were able to do it, all diligence and labor would cease, and man would devote himself to nothing else but this one thing,

# hidden Spirit of Nature.

...thing, and if people did not live rightly, the world would perish; for because of their greed and excess they would anger God. Since "no eye has seen, no ear has heard, nor has it entered into the heart of any man" <note>A paraphrase of 1 Corinthians 2:9, emphasizing the divine and secret nature of this substance.</note> what heaven has naturally embodied in this spirit, I have therefore briefly summarized some of the spirit's properties—which the **Philosophers** <note>Referring to the "Natural Philosophers" or alchemists who studied the secrets of matter.</note> have tested—to honor God, so that the pious might devoutly praise Him for His gifts, into whose hands this gift of God might come in the future. I wish herewith to indicate what virtue and power it has toward every being, and how it appears physically, so that it might be more easily discovered and recognized.

In its first essence, it appears in an earthly, unclean body filled with all manner of weakness, in which it has this property: it heals all wounds and corruption in the limbs of man, brings forth good flesh and consumes the rotten, cleanses all putrefaction, and takes away all stench—at whichever end it may dwell—healing everything internally and externally, and also creating beautiful skin.

In its second essence, it appears to the sight in a watery body, and is somewhat more beautiful than in the first, yet not much; this is because it still has its corruptions <term>corruptiones</term> <note>original: "Corruptiones." In alchemy, this refers to the impurities or the state of "decay" that a substance must go through before it can be purified.</note>, but it is more powerful in acting according to its virtues, much closer to the truth, and more effective in all works.

# Of the Revelation of the

The **influence of all heavenly bodies** <note>This refers to the alchemical belief in "astral influence," where the power of the stars and planets is "poured" into terrestrial substances.</note>, which can be studied in all of them and which are poured into every individual thing, are found in this spirit alone. This spirit is the **secret** <note>Original: "Geheimniß," often used in this context to mean a "mystery" or a hidden divine truth.</note> that has been hidden since the beginning, yet has been revealed to certain saints to whom it was granted by God, for the revelation of the riches of His glory. This spirit flies through the midst toward heaven like a rising morning cloud, carries its burning fire in water, and has its **clarified earth** <note>Original: "clarificirtes Erdreich." In alchemy, "clarification" is the process of purifying matter until it becomes transparent and perfect.</note> in heaven.

to be investigated, than through this. O you overflowing depth of the Wisdom of **GOD**! who has enclosed all that every body possesses within the power and strength of this single spirit. # On the Revelation of etc.

I heartily pray to those Christian believers who possess this knowledge, that they speak of it to no one, and communicate it to none except those who live god-fearing lives, and whom they have tested and recognized over a long period of time to live virtuously, praising and honoring **GOD**, who has given such a treasure to man; for many seek, but few find it, for the impure and those living in [carnal] pleasure are unworthy of this <term>science</term> <note>original: "Wissenschaft"; in this era, the term referred to any systematic body of knowledge or specialized skill, specifically the study of alchemy here.</note>. Therefore, this <term>art</term> <note>original: "Kunst"; often used by practitioners to refer to the "Great Work" of alchemy.</note> should only be shown to the god-fearing, because it is of a value that cannot be paid for. I testify before **GOD** that I do not lie, although it seems impossible to nature—namely, that there is no one now, nor has been, nor shall be, who has investigated nature so deeply.

Praised be the most high and almighty **GOD** who created this art, and whom it has pleased to reveal this knowledge to god-fearing men, Amen. Thus is completed the precious and excellent work, the **Revelation of the Hidden Spirit**, in which the secret and mystery of this world lie hidden. This spirit, however, is a unique divine and wonderful <term>Numen</term> <note>A Latin term for a divine power or presiding spirit that inhabits a place or object.</note>, a holy office; for it encompasses the whole world; in it is, will be, and was altogether <note>original: "m. e. a.", likely an abbreviation for "mit einander" (altogether) or a similar theological formula.</note>, and it truly overcomes the elements and the <term>fifth substance</term> <note>original: "fünfte Substanz"; also known as the *quintessence*, the pure celestial matter that was thought to compose the heavenly bodies and provide the vital spark in all things.</note>.

# THESAURUS
### TREASURY OF THE ALCHEMISTS.

Nature gives birth to a mineral in the bowels of the earth, of which there are two kinds that are found in many regions and places in Europe. The best, however, that has come into my hands and has been verified through experiments within the form of the macrocosm <note>original: *grossern Welt*, referring to the universe or outer world in contrast to the human microcosm</note>, is found in the rising of the Star of the Sphere of the Sun <note>original: *Astri Sphæræ Solis*</note>; the other is in the Southern Star <note>original: *Astro Meridionali*</note>. It is in its first bloom, which the Viscosity of the Earth <note>original: *Viscus Terræ*</note> presses forth through its star, and it is found to be red in its first coagulation. Within it lie enclosed all the flowers and colors of minerals, about which much has been written by the philosophers, claiming that it is of a cold and moist nature, and it has been compared to the element of water. 

But according to true knowledge and experiment, all philosophers before me have shot their arrows wide of the mark and missed the goal and target. they believed that Mercury and Sulphur were the sole mother of all metals. But they did not consider the third principle <note>likely referring to Salt, the third of the *Tria Prima* introduced by Paracelsus</note>, nor has any of them taken the fishing hook <note>original: *Fischernpern*</note> into their hand, which lies in the water. Yet, when the water is separated through the spagyric art <term>spagyric art|the alchemical practice of separating, purifying, and recombining the constituents of a substance</term>, the truth is revealed, which neither Galen <note>an influential ancient Greek physician whose theories were often challenged by alchemists</note> nor—

The author critiques previous philosophers for only recognizing Mercury and Sulphur as the origins of metals, neglecting a third essential principle found through spagyric separation.</summary>

# THE TREASURY OF THE TREASURIES OF THE ALCHEMISTS.
<note>original: *Thesaurus Thesaurorum Alchimistarum*</note>

Avicenna <note>a Persian polymath and one of the most influential physicians and philosophers of the Islamic Golden Age</note> never thought of this in his tattered fishing nets. And if I were only to describe to the current, highly learned physicians <note>original: *Physicis*</note> the name and the makeup, as well as the composition, dissolution, and coagulation <term>coagulation|the alchemical process of solidifying a liquid substance</term> which Nature demonstrated at the beginning of the world in her growth, I would have to write for a year, and many cow-hides <note>referring to the parchment or vellum used for manuscripts</note> would not suffice to instruct them.

I say, however, that three substances and materials are found in this mineral, namely: Mercury, Sulphur, and a mineral water, through which it is composed by Nature. These are resolved and dissolved through the spagyric art <term>spagyric art|the alchemical method of separating, purifying, and recombining the constituents of a substance</term> by its own sap, which is not yet quite ripe in its perfect autumn, just like a pear on a tree. The tree is the concealer of the pear, but when the heavenly stars <note>original: *Astra*</note> and Nature come together, it first puts forth its branches around the time of March; then its buds; following that, the buds open and show their blossoms, and so on until the ripening of the pear in the tree's autumn. It is the same with minerals, in that they are likewise first pressed forth in the bowels of the earth by the stars—which is to be highly considered by those alchemists who seek the Treasury of Treasuries <note>original: *Thesaurum Thesaurorum*</note>. To show them the way, the beginning, the middle, and the end, I will describe its own water, its own Sulphur, and its own balsam in this following treatise;

## On the Sulphur of Cinnabar
<note>original: *De Sulphure Cinabarino*</note>

Take cinnabar ore <note>a bright red mineral consisting of mercury sulfide, the primary ore of mercury</note> and treat it thus: boil it in rainwater in a stone vessel for three hours; afterwards, wash it out thoroughly. Dissolve it in an *Aqua Regis* <note>literally "Royal Water," a potent acid mixture; original: *Aqua Regis*</note> made from one part vitriol <note>iron or copper sulfate</note>, saltpeter, and sal ammoniac <note>ammonium chloride</note>. Then draw it off through the alembic <term>alembic|a distillation apparatus consisting of two vessels connected by a tube</term>, pour it back on top, and arrange for the pure to be separated from the impure. Then let it putrefy <term>putrefy|to undergo alchemical digestion or decomposition, often using gentle heat</term> in horse manure for one month. 

Afterwards, separate the elements in this way: when it gives its sign, begin to distill through the alembic with the first degree of fire <note>the lowest level of heat, often compared to the warmth of a brooding hen</note>, so that water and air ascend, while fire and earth remain at the bottom. Afterwards, put them back together, and when driven gently in the ash bath <note>a method of heating a vessel evenly by nesting it in a pan of warm ashes</note>, the elements of water and air will ascend once more, followed by the element of fire, which is recognized by an experienced artist. The earth is at the bottom of the vessel; search therein for that which has been sought by many, but found by few.

You shall skillfully prepare this dead earth <note>original: *Terram mortuam*; the solid residue remaining after distillation</note> in the reverberatory furnace <term>reverberatory furnace|a furnace that reflects heat onto the material being processed</term>, and thereafter proceed with the first degree of fire for five days and nights, and then continue in the same sealed manner at the second degree for another five days and nights. Thus, you will find a volatile salt <note>original: *Sal volatile*</note> like a delicate alkali <note>original: *Alcali*</note>,

<summary>This page provides a practical alchemical recipe for extracting a "volatile salt" from cinnabar ore through a cycle of acid dissolution, digestion in horse manure, and multi-stage distillation. The process focuses on the separation and refinement of the four classical elements to find a rare substance within the "dead" mineral residue.</summary>

# On the Red Lion.

Set it to be elevated <note>distilled or sublimated</note> so that it is exalted <note>purified and strengthened</note> and doubled in its degree. Then take the glass, still sealed, and place it in a cold location; there it does not dissolve <note>original: *solviret*</note> but rather coagulates. Set it to be elevated and coagulated again; do this a third time, and then the Tincture of the Sun <note>original: *Tinctura Solis*</note>

## On the Green Lion <note>original: *De Leone Viridi*</note>

Next, take the Vitriol of Venus <term>Vitriol of Venus|also known as copper sulfate; in alchemy, copper is associated with the planet Venus</term> prepared to the highest spagyric degree. Add to it the previously reserved elements of water and earth and dissolve it. Subject it to putrefaction <term>putrefaction|a process of slow digestion in a warm environment, intended to break down the "body" of the substance</term> for one month, as mentioned before. Once this has occurred, you will see the sign of the elements; separate these from one another. It will then appear to have two colors, white and red, with the red standing above the white. This red tincture of vitriol is so strong that it tints all white bodies into red, or all red bodies into white, and that is a marvel.

When this tincture is driven through the retorts <note>distillation vessels with long, downward-pointing necks</note>, you will see it turn into a blackness. Drive this again through the retorts, repeating the process until it comes over as a white vapor. Proceed and do not despair in your labor; rectify <term>rectify|to purify a substance, usually a liquid, through repeated distillation</term> it until you find the true, clear Green Lion. You will see that it is heavy in weight and possesses a coloring power; it is a tincture, a transparent gold. Continue until you see the sign and obtain it from the Green Lion, which cannot be purchased with all the treasure of the Roman Lion <note>likely a reference to the power and wealth of the Holy Roman Empire</note>. Blessed is he who finds it and can use it for the tincture.

This is the true balsam oil, a balsam of the heavenly stars <note>original: *astrorum*</note>, which allows no body to rot, nor permits any leprosy <note>original: *Lepram*</note>, or gout <note>original: *Poda-* likely *Podagra*</note>...

Woe unto your necks on the Day of Judgment! ### On the Green Lion.

I was born from her, and I follow after her: she knows me, and I know her as well. The light that is in her, I have seen within her, and I have also tested it externally in the figure of the *microcosm* <term>Microcosm; the "small world," referring to the human being as a reflection of the macrocosm or universe</term> and found it in her world.

But so that I may proceed further with my begun work and satisfy my students—to whom I gladly grant this, provided they are otherwise experienced in the light of nature, have good knowledge of the *stars* <term>Astra; in alchemy, these are the celestial influences or "inner stars" that give life and properties to minerals and plants</term>, and are learned in philosophy, which reveals the nature of all waters.

Take the described mineral *liquor* <note>liquid substance</note> at one-fourth the weight, two parts of the *red earth of the sun* <note>original: *Solis rubei terræ*; likely a refined or calcined form of gold</note>, and one part of the *sulfur of the sun* <note>original: *Sulphuris solis*; the essential "soul" or active principle of gold</note>; put them together in a *pelican* <term>Pelican; a circulatory distillation vessel with two side-arms that feed vapors back into the body, symbolizing self-refinement</term>, congeal it, and dissolve it—do this three times—and thus you have the *Tincture of the Alchemists* <note>original: *Tincturam Alchimistarum*</note>. Its weight is not described here, but it is reported in the *Book of Transmutation*.

He who now has one ounce of the *Star of the Sun* <note>original: *Astri Solis*</note> for a thousand ounces will also tinge its own *Body of the Sun* <note>original: *Corpus Solis*; physical gold</note>. If you have the *Star of Mercury* <note>original: *Astrum Mercurii*</note>, it likewise tinges the whole *Body of Mercury*. If you have the *Star of Venus* <note>original: *Astrum Veneris*</note>, it likewise tinges the whole *Body of Venus*. All of this shall likewise be confirmed and understood with the other planets or *stars*, namely *Saturn* <note>Lead</note>, *Jupiter* <note>Tin</note>, *Mars* <note>Iron</note>, and the *Moon* <note>Silver</note>; for tinctures are also made from them, which will not be written of further here, as there is enough in the <meta>catchword: Buch (Book)</meta>

### On the Green Lion.

...described in the *Book on the Nature of Things* <note>original: *De Natura Rerum*</note> and *Archidoxis*, and because I have here sufficiently indicated the *First Essence of Metals* <term>Primum Ens Metallorum; the primordial or fundamental substance from which all metals originate</term> and Minerals of the earth to the alchemists, so that they may possess the *Tincture of the Alchemists* <note>original: *Tincturam Alchimistarum*</note>.

This work here of the *Tincture of the Alchemists* is not intended to take nine months; rather, you may proceed with joy and without weariness in the *Spagyric Art* <term>Spagyria; the alchemical method of separating and recombining substances to purify them, often used in medicine</term> of the alchemists, and then—within forty alchemical days—fix, extract, exalt, putrefy, ferment, and coagulate this into the Stone, and so bring about the alchemical *Phoenix* <term>Phoenix; a symbol of the final, perfected stage of the Great Work, representing rebirth, immortality, and the completed Tincture</term>.

But it is well to note that the *Cinnabar-Sulfur* <note>original: *Sulphur Cinabarinus*; the active, fiery principle derived from mercury sulfide</note> is a volatile eagle, whose feathers fly without wind, and carry the body of the *Phoenix* to the Old One in his nest; there he is fed by the element of fire, and his young peck out his eyes. From this arises a *Whiteness* <note>the alchemical stage of *Albedo*, representing purification</note> that is separated within its *sphere* <note>original: *Sphæra*</note>, into the sphere and the life upon its heart, the balsam of its entrails, after the manner of the *Cabalists* <term>Cabalists; here referring to alchemists who used mystical, symbolic, or esoteric interpretations of nature</term>.

# Preface

### THEOPHRASTUS PARACELSUS,
### to all Alchemists and readers of this little book.
<note>original: "Theophrasti Paracelsi". The author is the famous Swiss physician and alchemist Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim (1493–1541).</note>

You lovers and experts of the <term>Alchemical art</term> <note>Alchemy: the historical practice of chemistry and speculative philosophy aimed at transmuting base metals into gold and finding a universal solvent or elixir.</note>, and all you who are eager to become rich through the great promise of making much gold and silver—as alchemy in many ways teaches and promises—and also you who will yet practice it, and allow yourselves to be <term>vexed</term> <note>The German "vexiren" refers to the "vexations" or trials of alchemy, where the practitioner is often frustrated or teased by the difficult nature of the materials and processes.</note>, and will not cease from this art until you learn what it gives you and how it keeps its great promises: I tell you that it is not so easy. It is the most difficult of all for the one who must investigate it and arrive at the results by himself. Daily experience makes it well known that among a thousand, not even one is granted the fulfillment of its promise.

<note>original vocabulary: Preface, Theophrastus Paracelsus, Alchemists, Alchemy, Gold, Silver</note>

<summary>In this preface, Paracelsus addresses aspiring alchemists with a stern warning, noting that despite the grand promises of wealth, the art of alchemy is incredibly difficult and rarely results in success for the practitioner.</summary>
<keywords>Paracelsus, Alchemy, gold, silver, alchemists, preface, transmutation</keywords>

Whether nature or art is to blame for this, I do not say; rather, it is one's own hindrance and the fault of the <term>laboratory worker's</term> <note>original: Laboranten. A shallow ceramic dish used for heating and refining metals.</note> <note>original: cum Arsenico. Latin for "with arsenic."</note> with <term>arsenic</term>) and such like.

Since all the signs of the heavens, the stars, and the <term>planetary characters</term> <note>The symbols representing planets and their associated metals, such as ☉ for the Sun/Gold or ☾ for the Moon/Silver.</note>, together with their convoluted words and names, and also all the recipes, materials, and tools of the craftsmen are well-known and familiar; it will not be necessary for this little book to bring forth and teach those same things anew; although it does not refrain from using such signs, names, and characters when it is necessary.

# Preface of Theophrastus Paracelsus, etc.

One should also know that these seven rules cannot be grasped in an instant <note>original: "im Huy"; an idiomatic expression meaning "in a flash" or "in the blink of an eye."</note> by a person of limited understanding upon the first reading and inspection. A simple intellect cannot endure weighty propositions; therefore, each rule requires thorough <term>disputation</term> <note>original: "disputirens"; refers to the formal practice of scholarly debate and dialectical analysis used to uncover the truth within a text.</note>. There are also many puffed-up and haughty individuals who imagine they understand it all too well and claim that what is written in this little book is worth nothing. They believe they know much more and better than this, and thus they utterly despise it (Sol <note>original symbol: ☉; the alchemical symbol for the Sun and Gold. In this context, it likely signifies the "golden" or "perfect" nature of the work, contrasted against its critics.</note>).

# The First Rule.

### On the Nature of Mercury and its Properties.

All things are hidden within all things; one among them all is their concealer and physical vessel—external, visible, and mobile. The flows <note>original: "Flüsse"; refers to substances in a liquid or molten state, or the principle of fluidity itself.</note> are all manifest in this vessel, for this vessel is a corporeal spirit; therefore, all coagulations <note>The process of a liquid becoming solid or semi-solid.</note> or solidifyings are trapped and enclosed within it, overcome, surrounded, and contained by the flow. No name can be found for this flow and its cause by which it might be called. And because there is no heat so great that it could equal it, it must be compared to the heat of the hellish fire <note>In alchemical literature, "hellish fire" often refers to a specific, extremely intense, or corrosive grade of heat used in transformations.</note>. Therefore, this flow has no communion with other flows that are melted by the heat of elemental, natural fire and become frozen, coagulated, and stiff through natural cold. They cannot do such things to Mercury <note>original: "Mercurio"; refers here to the alchemical principle of Mercury rather than just the common metal quicksilver.</note>; they are much too weak for him, and he cares nothing for them.

Therefore, it is to be noted that even the four mortal elemental forces have no power to act against the heavenly forces—which are also called the <term>Fifth Essence</term> <note>original: "Quintam essentiam"; the pure, indestructible "fifth element" (ether) that transcends the four terrestrial elements of earth, air, fire, and water.</note>, for the elements can neither add to nor take away from the <term>Fifth Essence</term> <note>original: "Quintæ essentiæ"</note>. The heavenly and hellish power is [to] the four elemen-

<summary>This page introduces the first rule of alchemy, describing Mercury as a "corporeal spirit" and a universal vessel containing all things, possessing a unique heat that transcends the four mortal elements.</summary>
<keywords>Mercury, alchemy, Fifth Essence, elements, coagulation, Paracelsus, First Rule</keywords>

...[ele]ments <note>Completing the word "elements" from the previous page's catchword "men-".</note> are not obedient. In this regard, it should be noted that no element, nor any elemental quality—whether it be dryness, moisture, heat, or cold—is capable of acting against the <term>Fifth Essence</term> <note>original: "Quintam essentiam"; the pure, celestial core of a substance that exists beyond the four physical elements.</note> or its power; rather, each has its effect only by and for itself.

## The Second Rule.
### Concerning Jupiter and its Nature.

Regarding that which is manifest—understand here the body of Jupiter <note>original: "Jovis"; in alchemical tradition, Jupiter is the name used for the metal tin.</note>—within it the other six physical metals are all hidden in a spiritual state, each one deeper and further removed than the last. Jupiter does not partake in the Fifth Essence, but rather belongs to the nature of the four elements; for this reason, its flow <note>original: "Fluß"; refers here to the state of being molten or liquid.</note> becomes apparent through even a small, incidental heat from fire, and its solidification <note>original: "Coagulation".</note> can likewise occur through a small, incidental cold. It also shares a commonality with all other metallic flows.

Therefore, the more closely one thing resembles another in its nature, the more readily it unites with it when they come into contact; a thing that is nearby is always more effective and more touching than that which is far away, for that which is distant is not burdensome. Nor does one fear that which is far off, even if it is of great size. In this same way, man has no great desire for the Kingdom of Heaven: the reason being that it is far from here, and has never been seen; likewise, one does not fear Hell very much, for it is far behind,

<meta>catchword: behind,</meta>

<summary>This page concludes the discussion on the resistance of the Fifth Essence to the elements and introduces the Second Rule concerning Jupiter (tin). It explains that Jupiter is governed by the four elements and uses a moral analogy to explain how distance affects human perception of spiritual reality.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, Jupiter, tin, Fifth Essence, elements, coagulation, molten state, heaven and hell, proximity</keywords>

--- Page 244 ---
<meta>Continuing from page 220, completing the thought regarding the "remoteness" of the Kingdom of Heaven and how distant things are often ignored.</meta>

<page-num>221</page-num>
<header>Of Jupiter and its Nature.</header>

...moteness <note>Completing the word "remoteness" from the previous page's catchword "nes."</note> behind, and no one knows or has seen its form, nor felt its pain; for that reason, it is immediately esteemed as nothing. Accordingly, distant things are little heeded, and indeed even rejected, when they are in a coarse place; for by the quality of the place, a thing is either corrupted or ennobled—a fact one could prove through many examples.

Therefore, the further Jupiter <note>The alchemical name for the metal tin.</note> is from Mars <note>original: "Marte"; the metal iron.</note> and Venus <note>original: "Venere"; the metal copper.</note>, and the closer it is to Sol <note>original: "Sole"; the metal gold.</note> and Luna <note>original: "Luna"; the metal silver.</note>, the more "golden" and "silvery" it becomes in its body: larger, stronger, more visible, more perceptible, more manifest—or more lovely and acceptable—as well as more recognizable, tangible, and truly perceived, than when it is at a distance.

Conversely, the further a thing is, the poorer and more disregarded it is in all the aforementioned respects; that which is present is always more noteworthy than that which is absent; the closer the visible is, the further the invisible. Therefore, the alchemist must be diligent to consider how you should bring and set Jupiter into the spiritual, heavenly, and distant place where Sol and Luna stand, and take whichever you wish of Sol and Luna from that distance and place them nearby in the spot where Jupiter stood physically. This must be done so that Sol and Luna are also physically present and stand truly before your eyes in the <term>assay</term> <note>original: "Prob"; a technical test to determine the purity or quality of a metal, usually by fire.</note>. For the metals are to be transformed from their imperfection into perfection through many kinds of recipes and labor: mixing one into the other, and again one...

<summary>This page explains the alchemical principle of "proximity," arguing that Jupiter (tin) gains the noble qualities of gold and silver as it is "moved" away from base metals. It instructs the alchemist to physically and spiritually transpose these metallic natures to achieve a state of perfection visible during an assay.</summary>
<keywords>Jupiter, tin, Sol, gold, Luna, silver, Mars, iron, Venus, copper, transmutation, alchemy, assay, perfection</keywords>

...ness <note>Continuing from the previous page to complete the word "remoteness."</note> from the other to separate clearly and purely is nothing else than an exchange and substitution through the proper work of alchemy <note>original: "Alchimey."</note>. Note: Gold contains much Jupiter <note>The alchemical name for tin.</note> and not a little Silver; add Saturn <note>The metal lead.</note> and Luna <note>The metal silver.</note> to it, and the Luna will increase from the others.

## The Third Rule.
### Of Mars and his Nature.

The six hidden metals have cast out the seventh metal from themselves and made it physical; they have also left it with the very lowest dignity, and imposed upon it the coarsest hardness and labor. In this metal, they have expelled and made manifest all their strength and hardness of <term>coagulation</term> <note>The process by which a liquid or "spiritual" substance becomes solid.</note>, while on the other hand withholding their color and flow along with their <term>nobility</term> <note>original: "Nobilität"; referring here to the purity and value of "noble" metals like gold.</note>. It is difficult and requires great effort to make a prince or a king out of an unworthy common man. But Mars <note>The metal iron.</note>, through his combativeness, also wins glory and places himself in the high station of kings. However, care is needed so that he is not captured in haste. One must consider by what means Mars may be brought to the royal station, and how Sol and Luna <note>Gold and silver.</note> might be brought with Saturn <note>Lead.</note> to the place of Mars.

The Fourth Rule. Of Venus etc. 223

## The Fourth Rule.
## Of the Property of Venus.

The other six metals have taken all their colors and their medium "flow" <note>The melting point or fluidity of the metal.</note>, together with their instability, and fashioned them into an external body for Venus <note>The alchemical name for the metal copper.</note>. It would be necessary, however, to prove to the understanding through several examples how one might make a visible thing invisible through fire, and the invisible visible and material. All combustible things are naturally transformed by fire from one form into another: into coals, soot, ashes, glass, colors, stone, and earth; and this earth may be brought back again into many new metallic bodies. And if one finds a burnt or "spoiled" <note>original: "verlegenens"; likely referring to metal that has become oxidized or degraded over time.</note> metal that is no longer malleable but brittle, and turns to dust when struck, or is fragile, one should anneal <note>original: "ausglühen"; the process of heating a metal and allowing it to cool slowly to remove internal stresses and restore ductility.</note> it well, and so it receives its malleability once again.

## The Sixth Rule.
### Of the Property of Silver <note>original: "Luna."</note>

If someone wanted to turn Silver into Lead or Iron, it would require just as much effort and labor as if you wanted to make Silver out of Mercury, Jupiter, Mars, Venus, or Saturn <note>In alchemy, these names refer to the seven metals: Mercury (Quicksilver), Jupiter (Tin), Mars (Iron), Venus (Copper), and Saturn (Lead).</note> for great profit and wealth. For it is not necessary to make bad things out of good, but rather to make good things out of bad. One must also know what kind of matter Silver is, or from where it comes; if someone does not know how to reflect upon this or inquire about it, it is impossible for him to make Silver. Question: What then is Silver? It is itself the seventh metal—external, physical, and material—composed of the six metals that are spiritually hidden within it. For the seventh always has the other six metals spiritually hidden within it, as has often been heard. Also, the six spiritual metals cannot exist without an external material metal; likewise, no physical metal can exist without the six spiritual ones, nor can they have a place within their essence. The seven physical metals are indeed often mixed or combined together, but this does not serve to make Gold <note>original: "Sol."</note> or Silver. After such mixing, each metal remains constant or volatile in the fire according to its own nature; take an example of this. Mix, as you wish, Mercury, Jupiter, Saturn, Mars,

# The Seventh Chapter.

...metals that are cold, if one wishes to bring them to flow—whether it be Jupiter <note>tin</note>, Venus <note>copper</note>, Saturn <note>lead</note>, Mars <note>iron</note>, the Moon <note>silver</note>, or the Sun <note>gold</note>—this must happen through the heat of fire. For with snow and ice, which are so cold, one cannot melt metals, but rather one hardens them. And when one brings metals to a flow with fire, as soon as the fire is removed from them, the cold enters into the metal, and it becomes hard, stiff, and dead immediately, remaining motionless. Since Mercury <note>original: "Mercurius"</note> is always in a state of flow and alive, tell me now: is it alive because of heat or cold?

If someone were to say it is of a cold and moist nature, and that it is alive because of cold: then such a person who says and believes this is not a knower of true nature, but is misled and deceived along with the common rabble. For the common rabble holds and believes only falsehoods about all things; therefore, one must withdraw from them if one wishes to rightly recognize the truth. For Mercury is not alive because of the cold, but from heat and a fiery nature, and indeed every living thing is fire. For heat is the cause of life, and cold is the cause of death. 

However, the fact that gold in itself is a pure fire, yet is not alive but hard, showing only the color of fire as a mixture of yellow and red within it, is because the fire or life within it is swallowed or <term>fixed</term> <note>original: "figiret"; rendered solid or stable</note> by the other cold metals. These five cold metals are: Jupiter, Mars, Saturn, Venus, and

# Of Sol <note>the Sun or Gold</note> and its Properties.

->the Moon,<- <note>original: "Luna"; in alchemy, the Moon represents the metal Silver</note> which gives to Gold from its virtues: from coldness, its body; from fire, its colors; from dryness, its hardening; from moisture, its weight; and from its brilliance, its luster. The reason why Gold is neither destroyed nor consumed in the element of earthly fire is the <term>stability</term> <note>original: "Beständigkeit"; this refers to the alchemical "fixity" of gold, meaning its ability to remain unchanged by heat</note> of the gold. One fire cannot burn or consume another; rather, when fire and fire meet, it only becomes greater and stronger in its effect. 

The heavenly fire that flows to us from the Sun, or is worked within the earth, is not a fire like that which is in the heavens, nor is it like our fire on Earth. Instead, the heavenly fire is for us a cold, rigid, and frozen fire, and this is the body of Gold. Therefore, one can win nothing from gold with our fire, except only that one separates it and makes it flow, just as the sun softens and makes snow and frozen water or ice flow. And for this reason, fire is not given the power to burn fire, because Gold itself is fire. In heaven it is dissolved <note>original: "resolvirt"</note>, but among us it is coagulated <note>original: "coagulirt"</note>. Gold exists in a threefold state of being:

# Added Pieces and Conclusions.

->God and Nature do nothing in vain.<- <note>original Latin: "Deus & Natura nihil frustra faciunt"</note>

The eternal place of all things, without time, without beginning and without end, is essentially present everywhere: It works even where there is no hope for it; that which is judged impossible, which is only unexpected, unbelievable, and utterly despaired of, will miraculously become true.

### Item. Now note the pieces regarding Living Mercury. <note>original Latin: "super Mercurium Vivum"; this refers to quicksilver or elemental mercury</note>

Everything that colors or makes things white is the life of nature, the property and power of light, which causes and creates life. For this movement, fire with its heat is its birth. And everything that colors or makes things black is the nature of death, the property and power of darkness, which causes death. For this solidification, the earth with its coldness is its <term>coagulation</term> <note>the process of a liquid becoming solid or semi-solid</note> and <term>fixation</term> <note>the process of making a volatile substance stable in fire</note>. The house is always dead, but the inhabitant is living fire. If you find how to use its examples correctly, you have won.

### Item, a proven flux-powder,
->Burn the fatty vervain.<- <note>original Latin: "Pingues adole Verbenas"; a quote from Virgil’s Eclogues, used here perhaps as a ritualistic or symbolic instruction for preparing the powder</note>

Take eight <term>Loth</term> <note>a historical Germanic unit of weight, approximately 14.6 grams</note> of saltpeter, four Loth of sulfur, and two Loth of <term>tartar</term> <note>potassium bitartrate, often called "cream of tartar," used in metallurgy as a fluxing agent</note>; mix them, and let it flow.

# Added Pieces and Conclusions.

...first make into <term>Luna</term> <note>original Latin: "Lunam"; referring to Silver</note>, or put much work into it with sublimation and other things; for it is only a waste of <term>Sol</term> <note>original Latin: "Solis"; referring to Gold</note> and Silver that is within it. There is indeed another much closer way, whereby <term>Mercury</term> <note>Quicksilver</note> becomes Silver, even with less cost, without all the effort of <term>coagulation</term> <note>the chemical process of solidifying a liquid or volatile substance</note>. 

Now, everyone would gladly read such pieces or "little arts" in the alchemical writings that are easy and quite simple to use, through which one could make much Gold and Silver in a short time. Such people have a dislike for many other writings and words that do not want to show them quickly, clearly, and plainly, and say how they should do it: "Do it thus and so," they would gladly hear, "so you have good Silver and Gold," from which they might become rich. 

Oh dear, wait a little longer until it is revealed to you in short words, without any effort or work in general, so that you could just snatch it in a <term>Huy</term> <note>a German idiomatic expression meaning "in a flash" or "instantly"</note>, and from that hour make Gold and Silver out of <term>Saturn</term> <note>Lead</note>, Mercury, and <term>Jove</term> <note>Tin</note>. It is and will never be so common as to be mastered or hit upon, however easy and slight it may be in itself. Gold and Silver are to be made by such an extremely small and simple trick and way of <term>alchemy</term> <note>the ancestral science of chemistry focused on transmutation and medicine</note> that it is not or would not be necessary to write or speak of any kind of teaching or book about it, no more than one needs to write about ready-made snow.

<summary>The author critiques the complexity of standard alchemical texts, suggesting that the true method of creating gold and silver is a simple "trick" that requires no elaborate books, though it remains hidden from those seeking only quick riches.</summary>

What then is the right way and brief foundation, which requires nothing difficult and so quickly becomes silver and gold that is true and right without any deception? How long will you delay in reporting such a thing? "I believe you know nothing of it yourself, but only make much useless, circuitous talk," someone might say. **Answer:** It has already been said, and is sufficiently evident in the <term>seven rules</term> <note>These likely refer to the "Seven Rules of the Planets" or fundamental laws of alchemy established earlier in the text.</note>. The **Art** is thus: When you cause the heaven or the <term>Sphere of Saturn</term> <note>original: "Sphæram Saturni." And so let it all run until the heaven of Saturn entirely disappears; then the planets remain standing alone, and have died with their old, perishable bodies, and have taken upon themselves a new, perfect, indestructible body. That same body is the **spirit of heaven**, from which the planets become bodily and alive again as before; take that same new body—

...both of a cold nature; from this, Mercury must freeze, solidify, and become hard.

Note: the coldness that Mercury requires for its solidification and killing <note>original: "Tödtung." In alchemical terms, "killing" a metal means taking away its "living" (liquid or volatile) state to make it solid or fixed.</note> is not something to be felt externally, like the cold of snow and ice; rather, it is felt more as warmth on the outside. In the same way, the heat of Mercury—which causes it to flow—is not an external heat felt in our hand, but is perceived externally more as a coldness. Regarding this, the <term>Sophists</term> <note>original: "Sophisten." Here, the author uses this to mean "presumed scholars" or those who practice false alchemy based on flawed logic.</note> say that it is of a cold and moist nature; therefore, they want to coagulate <note>original: "coaguliren." To turn a liquid into a solid or semi-solid state.</note> it only with hot things. This, however, draws it much more into a state of flux and keeps it there, rather than making it stand firm. One may learn all of this through practical testing. True alchemy, which alone learns the art from a single matter to make <term>Luna</term> <note>Silver.</note> or <term>Sol</term> <note>Gold.</note> from the five imperfect metals, uses no other recipe; rather, only from the metals, out of the metals, through the metals, and with the metals are the perfect metals made. For in other things there is Luna, and in metals there is Sol.

### What Alchemy is as an Undertaking.

Alchemy is merely an intention, a contemplation, and a clever reflection, by which one may transform the species of metals and bring them from one state and nature into another. Accordingly, anyone may well devise a good alchemical art through their senses and thoughts; for he who reflects well also strikes upon the art well and finds the truth.

Note: much weight is to be placed upon the <term>constellations</term> <note>original: "Gestirn." In this context, it refers to the spiritual influence of the stars and planets on earthly matter.</note> and upon the stones, for the constellation is the spirit and the formation of all stone. Every heavenly constellation, <term>Sun</term> <note>original: "Sol"</note> and <term>Moon</term> <note>original: "Luna"</note>, is also nothing but a stone in itself; and earthly stone has come from the heavenly stone, as its burning, coals, ashes, refuse, scouring, and purification, from which the heavenly stone separates itself, clear and pure

### ->Added Pieces and Conclusions.<-

**Coral**, is also like a stone, entirely red; it grows in the sea out of the nature of the water and air, in the form of wood or shrubs. Then it is transformed by the air, becomes stone-hard and entirely red, and is incombustible by fire, which is why it is called a stone. <note>It was a common ancient and medieval belief, popularized by Ovid and Pliny, that coral was a soft plant underwater that petrified instantly upon contact with the air.</note>

**Chalcedony**, is a stone of many clear and cloudy colors, also of mixed cloudy veins and liver-colors <note>original: "Leberfarben." This term was frequently used in historical mineralogy to describe a dull, reddish-brown hue resembling raw liver.</note>; it is the lowliest of the noble stones, shining forth with all colors.

**Topaz**, is a stone that also shines at night, and is found in other rocks. <note>The author likely refers to the "Topazos" of antiquity, which was often actually peridot, or refers to the belief that certain stones could emit "inner light" in the dark.</note>

**Amethyst**, is a stone appearing through red and yellow.

**Chrysoprase**, is a stone that appears fiery by night and golden by day. <note>original: "Chrysopasus." Historically, this name was applied to various greenish-gold gems; the "fiery at night" attribute suggests a confusion with other glowing stones in medieval lore.</note>

**Crystal**, is a stone—white, transparent, and like frozen water; it is <term>sublimated</term> <note>original: "sublimirt." In an alchemical sense, this refers to a substance being purified or elevated from a lower state to a higher, solid state.</note>, extracted, or, as one might say, grown from other rocks by air and cold. <note>The word "crystal" comes from the Greek "krystallos," meaning ice; it was long believed that rock crystal was ice that had frozen so hard it could never thaw.</note>

Thereafter, for a credible and thorough conclusion, note this departure: If anyone wishes to use correct senses, thoughts, and reason regarding metals—what they are and from whence they come—let them know that...

### ->Added Pieces and Conclusions.<-

Our metals are nothing other than the best part of common stones. They are the spirit of the stones—that is, the pitch, tallow, lard, oil, and fatness <note>original: "Pech, Unschlicht, Schmalz, Oel und Feißte." The author uses these visceral, fatty terms as alchemical metaphors for the combustible or "sulfurous" essence that was believed to give metals their shine and malleability.</note> of the stones. However, it is not good, pure, or perfect while it is still hidden and mixed within the stones; therefore, it must be sought and found within stones, recognized therein, and drawn out from them. That is, it must be forced, pressed, urged, and smelted; then it is no longer a stone, but a prepared, perfect metal, and it compares to the stars <term>celestial bodies</term> <note>original: "Gestirn des Himmels." In alchemical philosophy, the seven metals were believed to be terrestrial reflections of the seven "planets" or celestial bodies.</note> of heaven, which are also a separate kind of stone from this earthly rock.

Therefore, if someone wishes to seek ore or a mine, he must have such a calculation with him and be prepared; he must not be directed only toward the usual known ores, nor should he set his purpose only in the depths of the mountains to obtain good ore. For often, that which is outside in the daylight is exactly what is not in the depths of the earth, and often it is better and more plentiful than what is inside. Therefore, one should well observe and estimate every stone that one catches sight of—be it large or small, whole cliffs or pebbles <note>original: "Kißlingstein." Specifically refers to hard, rounded river stones or quartz-like pebbles.</note>—to see what nature and property it possesses. For often a pebble, which is neglected, is better than a "cow" <note>original: "Kuh." In historical German mining terminology, a *Kuh* (cow) was a slang term for a large, unproductive mass of rock or a barren part of a mineral vein that "ate" the profit.</note>. # Tetragrammaton.
<note>The Tetragrammaton refers to the four-letter Hebrew name of God (YHWH). In alchemical and Hermetic traditions, it represents the primordial power and the word through which the universe was spoken into existence.</note>

From eternity God was, and the second person of the Godhead is born from eternity from the Father, and from them both proceeds from eternity the third person, the Spirit of God; all three of equal power in a single, divine, inseparable essence, through which all things are created and sustained.

As the highest Trinity of God resolved in the divine, wise council to create the world, the angels, mankind, and in sum, all of nature: Thus God the Father created all things through the **Word** <note>referring to the Logos or the second person of the Trinity</note> as the second person—not into their final essence, but only as the **First Confused Matter** <note>original Latin: "Primam Materiam confusam." A reference to Genesis 1:2.</note>; likewise, it is called a thing in which all things lie hidden; and this **First Matter** was the water upon which the Spirit of God hovered.

The vital, spiritual essence that animates the physical elements of Earth, Air, Fire, and Water.</note>, the **pure & impure**, **Gold & Silver** <note>original Latin: "Aurum & Argentum." In this context, these likely represent the solar and lunar principles, or the "fixed" and "volatile" aspects of matter.</note>, light and darkness. In and out of this **Matrix**, which was surrounded by the light of God, the heavens and earth, air and water were created solely through the little word **LET IT BE** <note>original Latin: "FIAT." # Of Three Blessed Magical Things, etc.

To this I answer as follows: regarding the **Omnipotence of God** <term>Allmacht GOttes</term>, I must admit that it would have been quite possible for God to create a glorious body out of common earth. However, because God created all things—and in short, the entirety of **Nature** <term>Natur</term>—according to the wisest **Order** <term>Ordnung</term>, and not confusedly against the order of nature; and furthermore, since He maintains nature in its proper order and remains the Creator and Multiplier through that same nature until the end of the world (acting according to nature and not against it), it is even less possible that in the creation of man or his body, He acted against nature or made that noble body out of the coarsest elements of the earth.

I certainly admit that the form and appearance of the **Limbal Matter** <term>Materia Limbi</term> <note>original: "Materia Limbi." In alchemical and Paracelsian philosophy, this refers to the "primal slime" or the refined extract of the universe used to create Adam.</note> looked like earth, but for that reason it was not common earth. Rather, the most subtle essence of the entire **World-Machine** <term>Machinæ Mundi</term> <note>original: "Machinæ Mundi." A common Renaissance term for the universe viewed as a divinely crafted mechanism.</note> was brought together into one **Body** <term>Corpus</term>, which merely resembled the soil. For the Earth is a mother of all elements, and therefore she must be impregnated <note>In this context, "impregnated" or "made fertile" refers to the Earth receiving the essences of the other three elements (fire, air, water) to produce life.</note> before all others. Thus, it follows necessarily that her birth is called the **Microcosm** <term>Microcosmus</term> <note>original: "Microcosmus." Meaning "little world," this is the concept that the human being is a miniature map or reflection of the entire universe.</note>, that is, a new or small world, as a **Practical Philosopher** <term>Philosophus Practicus</term> <note>Likely referring to an alchemist or natural philosopher who learns through physical experimentation rather than just theory.</note> proves:

> Earth is the Mother of the Elements; from the Earth all things proceed, and to the earth all things shall return. <note>original Latin: "Terra est Mater Elementorum, de Terra procedit, & ad terram revertentur omnia."</note> Likewise—

<summary>The author argues that while Adam's body appeared to be made of "red earth," it was actually composed of a highly refined extract of the entire universe (the Microcosm). He asserts that God works through the wise order of Nature rather than by performing "confused" miracles against natural laws.</summary>
<keywords>Omnipotence of God, Nature, Materia Limbi, World-Machine, Microcosm, elemental theory, Adam's creation</keywords>

...after separation and impregnation, it shall ascend to heaven and return to its earth, and it shall possess the power of both the higher and the lower things, etc. <note>original Latin: "post separationem & imprægnationem ascendet in cœlum & revertetur in terram suam, & habebit vim superiorum & inferiorum &c." This is a central maxim from the *Emerald Tablet* (Tabula Smaragdina), a foundational text for alchemists which describes the unity of the universe.</note>

In summary, **God** has created all things according to the proper **Order** in nature, and nothing contrary to nature, with the exception of the **First Matter** <term>Primam Materiam</term> <note>In alchemy, the "First Matter" is the original, formless substance required to create the Philosopher's Stone or the universe itself.</note>, out of which everything else was subsequently created. This same matter was made out of nothing, which is why it is also called the **Abyss** <term>Abyssus</term>, that is, the **Womb of the World** <term>Matrix Mundi</term> <note>original: "Matrix Mundi." A term used to describe the universal mother-substance or "womb" from which all physical forms emerge.</note>, or the **Fire**, **Soul**, etc., from which everything was then created; this same matter was created or made out of nothing.

If God had intended to create something contrary to nature out of this **Matter** or the **Water** upon which the **Spirit of God** hovered, He would not have required six days to bring forth one thing after another according to the proper order of nature. Instead, He could certainly have created all creatures in an instant outside of the water. But God did not wish to make spirits, but rather natural creatures. For this reason, He first took the "chest" for Himself, in which the treasure of all nature lay mixed together in a single mass; that was the **Eternal Fire**, which has neither beginning nor end. Through the hovering Spirit of God, this mass was quite clearly separated—the coarse from the subtle, the light from darkness—and each was ordered into its proper place quite clearly, where it remains until the

For when a foreign, animal mother receives the human seed with lust, a human body results from it, though it commonly bears some foreign sign or mark.

Thus, the creation of heaven and earth was nothing other than the **First Matter or Confused Mass** <term>Prima Materia seu Massa confusa</term> <note>The primordial, chaotic substance from which the universe was fashioned by the Divine.</note>. # Of Three Blessed Magicians, etc. #
